Original statements and key identity links remain open. <a href="#evidence/what-has-actually-been-read">Source scope ↗</a></p></aside><div class="contents-main"><div class="index-label"><h2>Contents</h2><span>08 CHAPTERS</span></div><div class="contents-rows"><a class="contents-row" href="#game"><span>01</span><h2>Game</h2><p>Player role and the two structures to test.</p><span aria-hidden="true">↗</span></a><a class="contents-row" href="#episode"><span>02</span><h2>First episode</h2><p>One campaign, one payment conflict, fifteen minutes.</p><span aria-hidden="true">↗</span></a><a class="contents-row" href="#objects"><span>03</span><h2>Objects &amp; Conflicting records become part of play when they change a decision about the work.</p>
</div><nav class="page-links" aria-label="In Game"><a href="#game/player-role"><span>01</span>Player role</a><a href="#game/two-structures-to-test"><span>02</span>Two structures to test</a><a href="#game/how-the-story-enters-play"><span>03</span>How the story enters play</a><a href="#game/design-decisions"><span>04</span>Design decisions</a></nav><div class="release-note">DESIGN 0.10 / ART 0.9<br><a href="previews/index.html">Current previews ↗</a><br><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">Object catalogue ↗</a><br><a href="archive/index.html">Archive ↗</a></div></aside><div class="chapter-main"><article class="article-body"><figure class="plate editorial-plate" id="fig-D01"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">D01</span><strong>A place to operate; records to inspect</strong></figcaption><a class="scene-preview" href="#art/world-and-interface"><img src="assets/art-system/campus.png" alt="Current office-derived park: three sites around shared courts, paths and original landmarks" class="pixel-preview" loading="lazy"><span>Explore the working art study ↗</span></a><div class="example-pair"><div><b>Built</b><p>Site selection, cutaways, courtyard layouts, landmark inspectors and linked practice records.</p></div><div><b>Next playable proof</b><p>Reserve capacity, run one handoff and see how the chosen plan changes delivery.</p><a href="#production/production-sequence">Build sequence →</a></div></div><div class="figure-note">Current art and interaction proof. The arrangement is fictional; the production economy has not been implemented.</div></figure>

<h2 id="game/player-role"><a class="section-anchor" href="#game/player-role">Player role</a></h2>
<p>The working proposal gives the player a fictional operations role with limited authority. They can make production plans, reserve capacity and request approvals. Historical scenes use fixed records and distinguish what the source reports from what has been independently checked.</p>
<p>The audience is provisionally curious adults familiar with management games, without accounting expertise. Desktop browser; mouse and keyboard; pauseable single-player sessions. Test a 12–15 minute episode before setting campaign length. Audience, player authority and distribution remain open.</p>
<h2 id="game/two-structures-to-test"><a class="section-anchor" href="#game/two-structures-to-test">Two structures to test</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th></th>
<th>Operating campus</th>
<th>Reconstruction park</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Player action</td>
<td>Arrange production and make authorized commitments</td>
<td>Arrange a reconstruction and test explanations</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Immediate problem</td>
<td>Preserve delivery while a payment is unresolved</td>
<td>Explain what the available payment records establish</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Can change</td>
<td>Fictional plans, schedules and outcomes</td>
<td>Selection, comparisons and the account assembled by the player</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Cannot change</td>
<td>The historical record</td>
<td>Past events or missing endpoints</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Main risk</td>
<td>The player assumes real people had the same freedom or information</td>
<td>The activity becomes filing rather than play</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Test both with the same returned-payment episode and amount of source information. Look for voluntary experimentation and accurate explanation. The current preference is operating play with a linked historical reconstruction; it remains a testable choice.</p>
<figure class="plate" id="fig-01"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">FIG. 01</span><h2 id="game/how-the-story-enters-play"><a class="section-anchor" href="#game/how-the-story-enters-play">How the story enters play</a></h2></figcaption><div class="figure-body"><div class="lanes"><div class="lane-label">Practice<span>Invented conditions</span></div><div class="lane"><b>Plan</b><span>→</span><b>Run</b><span>→</span><b>Respond</b><span>→</span><b>Review outcome</b></div><div class="lane-label fixed">History<span>Fixed records</span></div><div class="lane historical"><span class="empty-track"></span><div><b>Compare the mechanism</b><small>What does the source establish?</small></div><a href="#evidence/source-classes">Open supporting records ↗</a></div></div></div><div class="figure-note">The player's run changes the practice outcome. The historical comparison retains its own dates, permissions and source limits.</div></figure>

<p>Every narrative chapter includes a short historical comparison. It identifies what transfers from practice, what the source says, and what remains unresolved. The deeper dossier is optional. A separate sandbox may omit the historical sequence.</p>
<h2 id="game/design-decisions"><a class="section-anchor" href="#game/design-decisions">Design decisions</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Proposed rule</th>
<th>Reason</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Separate work, obligations, cash, records and permissions</td>
<td>The story depends on their differences</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Persistent staff departments; dated supplier engagements</td>
<td>Employment and external work create different commitments</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Projects are configurable production clusters</td>
<td>Placement and staffing should affect production</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Buildings provide capacity, access or a burden</td>
<td>Geography must have a function</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Preserve selection and period across views</td>
<td>Players need to compare the same object</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Keep historical and simulated outcomes distinct</td>
<td>A player&#39;s choices cannot create evidence</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Open decisions: audience; player authority; real-name representation; final building aesthetics; game distribution. This design review is prepared for public GitHub Pages; publication of the eventual game remains a separate decision.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#contents"><small>Previous</small>Contents</a><a href="#episode"><small>Next</small>First episode →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-episode" hidden aria-labelledby="title-episode"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">CHAPTER / 02</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-episode" tabindex="-1">First episode</h1></header><div class="chapter-intro"><p>A $100,000 campaign needs a $12,000 fabricated component. The bill says Paid, but the available bank record shows a debit followed by an equal return. The player must protect delivery while confirmation is pending.</p>
<p>All amounts, schedules and choices in this prototype are invented.</p>

The final historical interaction requires native exhibits before implementation.</div></figure>

</svg></div></div><div class="figure-note">C-07 / O-19 · Invented USD example. A returned attempt and a surviving Paid label do not establish the later recipient endpoint.</div></figure>

<p>The workbench opens from the affected project or obligation. It separates terms, payment events and platform records. The player can request processor history or recipient confirmation, then reserve substitute capacity, defer a handoff or change the production sequence. Confirmation may remain pending.</p>
<p>Inspecting and comparing are navigation actions. The consequential choice is the changed commitment or plan they inform.</p>
<h2 id="episode/historical-comparison"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/historical-comparison">Historical comparison</a></h2>
<p>Creative Peaks on CASE-01 p.34 remains the proposed historical anchor. The first binding pass has inspected BILL, bank-data and QBO export rows. A return-date conflict, a bank-direction conflict and an incorrect derived vendor join require explicit treatment. Historical play remains inactive while those links and an ordinary pooled-payment control are checked.</p>
<figure class="plate art-plate" id="fig-B01"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">B01</span><strong>First source binding</strong></figcaption><div class="binding-status"><strong>Creative Peaks · invoice 8</strong><p>Export fields inspected. Original statements, complete payment links and recipient receipt remain open.</p></div><div class="binding-track"><div class="binding-event"><small>20 OCT 2024 / REPRESENTATION</small><h3 id="episode/payment-created"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/payment-created">Payment created</a></h3><b class="binding-amount">$23,500</b><p>BILL creation date and QBO payment entry. This is a recorded payment representation.</p></div><div class="binding-event"><small>21 OCT 2024 / FUNDING</small><h3 id="episode/bank-extraction-debit"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/bank-extraction-debit">Bank extraction: debit</a></h3><b class="binding-amount">−$23,500</b><p>Names Creative Peaks and invoice 8. BILL funding export also records a debit.</p></div><div class="binding-event conflict"><small>22 OCT 2024 / CANDIDATE RETURN</small><h3 id="episode/bank-extraction-credit"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/bank-extraction-credit">Bank extraction: credit</a></h3><b class="binding-amount">+$23,500</b><p>BILL dates a matching return October 22; CASE-01 p.31 says October 21. The bank credit lacks the payment’s token. Its unique link to this attempt remains unresolved.</p></div></div><div class="binding-compare"><div><h3 id="episode/later-platform-fields"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/later-platform-fields">Later platform fields</a></h3><p><b>Paid / PaidInFull</b> appear in later exports. That establishes a status observation, not an uninterrupted history or final supplier receipt.</p></div><div><h3 id="episode/23-october-is-a-separate-record"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/23-october-is-a-separate-record">23 October is a separate record</a></h3><p>QBO credits Chase and debits clearing account 1072. The Chase extraction labels the matching item <b>IN / DEPOSIT</b>. No bank-direction arrow is justified yet.</p></div></div><div class="binding-records"><a href="../The_Zero_Machine_Specific_Story_Reconstruction%20FINAL.pdf#page=34" target="_blank"><img src="assets/zero-machine-page-34.png" alt="Actual page 34 of The Zero Machine reconstruction" loading="lazy"><span><b>The reconstruction</b><small>CASE-01 / P.34<br>Open source page ↗</small></span></a><div><h3 id="episode/the-ordinary-control-is-still-open"><a class="section-anchor" href="#episode/the-ordinary-control-is-still-open">The ordinary control is still open</a></h3><p>Two BILL allocation rows total $4,520 and share an ACH confirmation with one bank-data debit. This supports the pooled-funding comparison at export level; original cents, group coverage and recipient settlement remain unchecked.</p></div></div><nav class="binding-links" aria-label="Source binding documents"><a href="#binding">Read the binding packet →</a><a href="#binding-inventory">Inspect files and exact rows →</a><a href="#production/source-review">Review rules →</a></nav><div class="figure-note">Dollar notation follows the source. Native currency, identifier-complete joins and final receipt are not established here. The fictional $12,000 practice scene remains separate.</div></figure>

<div class="source-comparison"><a href="../The_Zero_Machine_Specific_Story_Reconstruction%20FINAL.pdf#page=34" target="_blank" class="source-excerpt"><span class="excerpt-page"><img src="assets/zero-machine-page-34.png" alt="Actual page 34 of The Zero Machine" loading="lazy"></span><span class="excerpt-caption"><small>CASE-01 / PAGE 34</small><strong>Creative Peaks</strong><span>Payment state and bank route</span><b>Open source page ↗</b></span></a><a href="../The_Zero_Machine_Specific_Story_Reconstruction%20FINAL.pdf#page=45" target="_blank" class="source-excerpt"><span class="excerpt-page"><img src="assets/zero-machine-page-45.png" alt="Actual page 45 of The Zero Machine" loading="lazy"></span><span class="excerpt-caption"><small>CASE-01 / PAGE 45</small><strong>Legitimate pooling controls</strong><span>Worked chains · continues on p.46</span><b>Open source page ↗</b></span></a></div>

<p>Ask the player to distinguish what their invented run changed from what the historical records establish. If this transition feels detached from the operating problem, revise it before expanding the campaign. See <a href="#evidence/issues-found-during-this-pass">source issues</a>.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#game"><small>Previous</small>Game</a><a href="#objects"><small>Next</small>Objects &amp; systems →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-objects" hidden aria-labelledby="title-objects"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">CHAPTER / 03</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-objects" tabindex="-1">Objects &amp; systems</h1></header><div class="chapter-intro"><p>A project connects people, contracts, places and obligations. These are separate objects even when they share a building or illustration.</p>

<h2 id="objects/object-definitions"><a class="section-anchor" href="#objects/object-definitions">Object definitions</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Object</th>
<th>Essential relationships</th>
<th>What it becomes on screen</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Person</td>
<td>Roles and employment/engagements over time; attributed actions</td>
<td>Worker, visitor, principal or account-linked subject</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Employment</td>
<td>Employer, department, availability, compensation, benefits, period</td>
<td>Persistent park staff in creative, production, accounts, media, ops</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Supplier engagement</td>
<td>Supplier, service, milestone, rate, terms, obligation</td>
<td>Visiting specialist, crew, delivery or external service</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client relationship</td>
<td>Contracting entity and agreements</td>
<td>Client contact and a portfolio of commissions</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contract</td>
<td>Scope, fees, outside costs, approvals, rights, timing</td>
<td>Offer and terms; creates commitments, not immediate cash</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Project</td>
<td>Deliverables, dependencies, labor and supplier requirements</td>
<td>Temporary, configurable production cluster</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Site</td>
<td>Address, occupancy, facilities, lessee and lease</td>
<td>Office/studio/district with capacity and cost</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Legal entity</td>
<td>Owners, accounts, contracts, debts, rights</td>
<td>Selectable boundary; may own or occupy several sites</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Obligation</td>
<td>Debtor, creditor, currency, amount, due date, basis</td>
<td>A durable commitment, independent of a platform label</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment attempt</td>
<td>Funding, route, batch, profile, return/reissue and settlement</td>
<td>A dated lifecycle in the payment workbench</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>System account/profile</td>
<td>Platform identity, versions, privileges</td>
<td>A software/control object, not automatically a person</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission</td>
<td>Who may do what, where, when, granted by whom</td>
<td>Control edges and precise blocked-action explanations</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record/version</td>
<td>Provenance, author/account, date, immutable source and prior versions</td>
<td>Receipt, message, audit event or comparison</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Claim</td>
<td>Exact proposition, scope, supporting/conflicting records, proof boundary and review state</td>
<td>Attributed finding or open question; many records may support one claim</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Employment can be salaried, hourly or otherwise structured. Fixed recurring cost is an initial simulation approximation. Benefits create obligations; a repeat freelancer remains an external engagement unless the underlying relationship changes.</p>
<p>Clients and suppliers may share animation assets but have different behavior. A client commissions work; a supplier provides it. Courier engagements attach to handoffs. Artist collaborations may include rights as well as production. A freelancer&#39;s tax form is a dated reporting artifact, with treatment dependent on the recipient, payment route and year; see <a href="https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-prior/i1099mec--2024.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener">IRS 2024 instructions</a>. It is not the character type.</p>
<h2 id="objects/sites-and-companies"><a class="section-anchor" href="#objects/sites-and-companies">Sites and companies</a></h2>
<p>“Office Space” can be a build-menu category. Each site still needs an occupant, lessee or owner, dates, capacity, cost and narrative purpose. McKibbin, Boerum, 266 Johnson, Colorado, Porter and Hong Kong require those checks before becoming map locations.</p>
<p>An affiliated company such as Starfish is an entity with contracts, accounts and obligations. It may share a site or remain off-map. Place a site only when location, capacity, access or geography matters; otherwise show an address in the inspector.</p>
<figure class="plate" id="fig-05"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">FIG. 05</span><h2 id="objects/operating-loop"><a class="section-anchor" href="#objects/operating-loop">Operating loop</a></h2></figcaption><div class="figure-body"><div class="clock-diagram"><div class="clock-row"><b>Work</b><div class="flow">Scope <i>→</i> Capacity <i>→</i> Production <i>→</i> Acceptance <i>→</i> Review</div></div><div class="clock-row"><b>Commitments</b><div class="flow commitments"><span>Deposits</span><span>Payroll</span><span>Supplier milestones</span><span>Invoices due</span></div></div><div class="clock-row"><b>Settlement</b><div class="flow settlements"><span>Client receipts</span><span>Outflows</span><span>Returns / reissues</span></div></div></div></div><div class="figure-note">Positions indicate separate processes, not a payment schedule. A deposit, payroll or media payment may precede delivery or client collection.</div></figure>

<p>Accepting scope reserves future time. A specialist may reduce production time but require a deposit and advance notice. Internal production competes for shared staff and facilities. Acceptance may unlock a contractual milestone; it does not settle an invoice.</p>
<p>Supported routine transactions are handled in batches. Inquiry starts with a conflict that affects a commitment or historical question: inspect → compare → request a useful record → narrow the explanation → adjust the plan.</p>
<h2 id="objects/economy"><a class="section-anchor" href="#objects/economy">Economy</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>System</th>
<th>Player input</th>
<th>Consequence</th>
<th>Hard boundary</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Capacity</td>
<td>Assign people, reserve facilities, accept scope</td>
<td>Work advances or queues</td>
<td>Work activity is not recognized revenue</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contracts</td>
<td>Choose eligible scope/production plan, seek approval</td>
<td>Rights and obligations change</td>
<td>Budget ceiling is not a cash receipt</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Treasury</td>
<td>Schedule authorized payments and contingency</td>
<td>Reservations, posted bank movement and final settlement affect different liquidity measures</td>
<td>Posted cash, committed funds, usable liquidity, credit and revenue remain separate</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Allocate work, add slack, commission help</td>
<td>Availability and service continuity change</td>
<td>No invented emotional diagnosis of real people</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Suppliers</td>
<td>Commit service, approve milestones, check delivery</td>
<td>Payables and production dependencies</td>
<td>Real invoice does not close payment endpoint</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment lifecycle</td>
<td>Inspect attempts, returns, reissues, confirmation</td>
<td>An obligation may remain unresolved</td>
<td>Paid is not a universal settlement truth</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Reporting</td>
<td>Compare eligible views and periods</td>
<td>Same work can have different representations</td>
<td>A noncash reclassification cannot refill a bank</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Control</td>
<td>Inspect/request specific permissions</td>
<td>A supported action becomes available or remains blocked</td>
<td>Ownership and seniority do not grant root access</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Records</td>
<td>Preserve versions, compare sources, bind claims</td>
<td>Better explanation and retained lineage</td>
<td>Missing records do not become adverse facts by default</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Start with one cash balance, five department capacities, one site and a few external commitments. Add a credit facility, currencies or legal entities when the test needs them. Hong Kong data requires explicit HKD/USD fields and sourced conversion rules.</p>
<p>Compute money in integer minor units. Preserve original source precision and record display rounding separately. Aggregate by stated population and economic episode; a platform object and its bank member cannot become two expenses.</p>
<h2 id="objects/customization-and-outcomes"><a class="section-anchor" href="#objects/customization-and-outcomes">Customization and outcomes</a></h2>
<p>Players can vary facility mix, project placement, department assignments, make/buy choices, schedule slack, contingency reservations and saved production plans. Desk arrangements, document pins, filters and fictional decoration provide expression. Physical distance affects a physical handoff only when modeled; it does not accelerate bank authorization.</p>
<p>Show commitments met, production completed, cash remaining, unresolved obligations and workload strain in the fictional model. Evaluate supported explanations separately. Weighting these into one score is an open design choice.</p>
<p>Allow pause, planning undo, branching and replay. After running an interval, show the action and its consequence. The historical company can close even when the player has successfully understood a system failure. No fraud score or suspect-ranking mechanic is proposed.</p>

Keep the full chronology available in the record view.</p>

<h2 id="narrative/chapter-sequence"><a class="section-anchor" href="#narrative/chapter-sequence">Chapter sequence</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Chapter proposal</th>
<th>First experience</th>
<th>New distinction</th>
<th>Historical anchor in CASE-01</th>
<th>Requires before script lock</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>1. The work</td>
<td>Deliver a small production using staff and a supplier</td>
<td>Work / cost / fee / receipt</td>
<td>pp.1–6, 56</td>
<td>One complete contract/job chain</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2. The desk</td>
<td>Responsibility without the permission to finish a task</td>
<td>Role / access / authority</td>
<td>pp.10–16</td>
<td>Dated access and correspondence exhibits</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3. The report</td>
<td>A busy campus has two different financial portraits</td>
<td>Activity / presentation</td>
<td>pp.17–22</td>
<td>Native workbooks and audience versions</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4. The payment</td>
<td>A label and cash lifecycle disagree</td>
<td>Obligation / attempt / settlement</td>
<td>pp.28–29, 33–34</td>
<td>Adverse chain plus legitimate controls</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5. The other company</td>
<td>Cash travels while a debt stays</td>
<td>Entity / site / benefit / burden</td>
<td>pp.23–27, 48–49</td>
<td>Corrected property/affiliate source manifest</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>6. The handover</td>
<td>New ownership, incomplete control, changed records</td>
<td>Ownership / administration / version</td>
<td>pp.29–38</td>
<td>Settlement, platform and migration records</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>7. After closing</td>
<td>The world stops producing; documents continue changing</td>
<td>Operational time / record time</td>
<td>pp.39–40</td>
<td>Bounded audit windows and account attribution</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Each chapter begins with a normal case, allows a plan, introduces pressure, offers competing readings and resolves only what the records support. Documents enter when they help answer the current question.</p>
<h2 id="narrative/events"><a class="section-anchor" href="#narrative/events">Events</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Event</th>
<th>Decision or record interaction</th>
<th>Consequence and narrative purpose</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>A commission exceeds the current workshop</td>
<td>Internal overtime/slack versus external specialist versus phased delivery</td>
<td>Establishes the attraction of growth and the cost of commitments</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client approval arrives after a reserved slot</td>
<td>Hold capacity, swap sequence, or release it</td>
<td>Makes contract time and production time diverge</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A real bill enters a pooled payment</td>
<td>Inspect batch membership or let supported routine processing proceed</td>
<td>Teaches a normal state before an exception</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A returned attempt remains labelled Paid</td>
<td>Protect delivery while seeking the right confirmation</td>
<td>Changes operations without requiring an accusation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Two transfers share amount and date</td>
<td>Compare native identities and banks</td>
<td>Defeats the tempting but invalid matching shortcut</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>An off-map affiliate requests liquidity</td>
<td>Compare authorization, return terms and remaining commitments</td>
<td>Distinguishes gross deployment from retained benefit</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A report preserves profit but changes scale</td>
<td>Pin operating activity beside two reports</td>
<td>Makes representation consequential and inspectable</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The owner cannot administer the software</td>
<td>Follow dated permission grants</td>
<td>Reveals the distance between ownership and control</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Migration keeps money fields and loses job identity</td>
<td>Restore or mark lineage using documented links</td>
<td>A puzzle about history, not “missing money” animation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Business closes but an account edits a bill</td>
<td>Compare operation date with edit date</td>
<td>Ends the productive world without inventing an evidentiary ending</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>These are proposed decisions and record interactions. Compare, pin and follow do not count as consequential gameplay by themselves. In an operating scenario they must inform a commitment; in reconstruction they must help discriminate between explanations.</p>
<p>Relief, tax and employee-benefit episodes are deferred modules. Their historical rules, populations and source claims require specialist review before gameplay design.</p>
<h2 id="narrative/characters-and-voice"><a class="section-anchor" href="#narrative/characters-and-voice">Characters and voice</a></h2>
<p>Use fictional roles with practical constraints: a producer coordinating dependencies; an account lead protecting a promise; a freelancer awaiting confirmation; an operations coordinator finding capacity; a finance approver with a specific permission; a client contact authorized to approve scope. One person can occupy several roles.</p>
<p>Historical representation follows documented roles and actions. Motives and dialogue require sources. Rogers&#39;s authority problem must not become an invented suspicion motive, and named account activity must not become authenticated human action. The <a href="#evidence/cast-organizations-and-authority-over-time">cast register</a> preserves those distinctions.</p>
<p>Dry humor can come from fictional workplace friction. Avoid jokes at the expense of unpaid people and invented quotations from source subjects.</p>
<p><strong>Source task — message discovery:</strong> obtain the original “no yappin” record, its sender, date and context before scripting that encounter.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#objects"><small>Previous</small>Objects &amp; systems</a><a href="#interface"><small>Next</small>Interface →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-interface" hidden aria-labelledby="title-interface"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">CHAPTER / 05</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-interface" tabindex="-1">Interface</h1></header><div class="chapter-intro"><p>Keep the world, selected object and active question in view. Documents open alongside the context that made them useful.</p>

<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>View</th>
<th>Question</th>
<th>Primary marks</th>
<th>Prohibited inference</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Work</td>
<td>What is happening; what blocks it?</td>
<td>Project stages, capacity, dependencies</td>
<td>Activity equals revenue</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Cash</td>
<td>What moved and how far is it supported?</td>
<td>Bank events, attempts, returns, known endpoints</td>
<td>Paid equals settled; batch equals beneficiary</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Information</td>
<td>Which record, which version, from where?</td>
<td>Lineage, comparisons, missing links</td>
<td>Failed export equals destruction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Control</td>
<td>Who could do which action then?</td>
<td>Dated grants, approval chains, system boundaries</td>
<td>Capability proves execution</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Who supplies work and bears interruption?</td>
<td>Department commitments, employment/engagement links</td>
<td>Model strain proves a real person&#39;s feelings</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Rights and obligations stay in the inspector across views. Preserve the selected object, camera and period. Show event date, record version and availability to the represented role separately. Later discoveries belong to retrospective inspection. Available, received, read and understood are different states; unknown is valid.</p>
<figure class="plate" id="fig-07"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">FIG. 07</span><h2 id="interface/desk-layouts"><a class="section-anchor" href="#interface/desk-layouts">Desk context</p></div></div></div></figure>

<p>Start with two pinned comparisons and one active inspector. Reading pauses the world and retains a return target. A full window manager is outside the first prototype.</p>
<h2 id="interface/document-vocabulary"><a class="section-anchor" href="#interface/document-vocabulary">Document vocabulary</a></h2>
<p>The <a href="#art/interface-materials">interactive material study</a> develops the invoice, saved record, bank-event strip, form reader, player note and record index as distinct objects. The same components open from the park inspector. White invoice stock carries obligations; pink carbon stock carries a version; the narrow strip carries dated events. Shape and printed labels carry the distinction as well as colour.</p>
<h2 id="interface/payment-workbench"><a class="section-anchor" href="#interface/payment-workbench">Payment workbench</a></h2>
<p>Use three aligned columns: obligation and terms; bank/processor lifecycle; platform label and version. Stable IDs connect the records. Open it directly from a bill or project; a finance building may also provide access and represent staff-review capacity. The permission model controls actions regardless of entry point.</p>
<p>See the <a href="#episode/payment-states">payment lifecycle</a> and the <a href="#research-ux">detailed interface research</a> for the same interaction at different levels of detail.</p>
<h2 id="interface/quantities-and-evidence"><a class="section-anchor" href="#interface/quantities-and-evidence">Quantities and evidence</a></h2>
<p>Every quantitative display includes measure, unit, population, period and basis. Keep necessary qualifiers visible; put the exact source locator one step away. Distinguish zero, not applicable, unavailable, not supplied, not inspected and modeled.</p>
<p>A transfer animation ends at the last supported endpoint. Bank movement, information transfer and permission edges use different marks. A named account, job title or access grant cannot stand in for a person&#39;s action.</p>
<h2 id="interface/accessibility"><a class="section-anchor" href="#interface/accessibility">Accessibility</a></h2>
<p>Keyboard equivalents; visible focus; a nonspatial object list; independent text scaling; labels and patterns for status; reading pause; reduced motion; adjustable audio; no hover-only evidence; reliable back history. At small widths, switch between world and inspector with an explicit return. Mobile reading is an initial target; full mobile management controls are not yet scoped.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#narrative"><small>Previous</small>Narrative</a><a href="#art"><small>Next</small>Art &amp; sound →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-art" hidden aria-labelledby="title-art"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">CHAPTER / 06</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-art" tabindex="-1">Art &amp; sound</h1></header><div class="chapter-intro"><p>One original pixel system, developed from components into a working scene. RCT supplies the scale and spatial logic; the agency supplies the buildings, work, records and people. The document keeps its own crisp typography.</p>

Tall window grids, recessed doors and a concrete street edge.</p><a class="office-credit" href="https://mattfryed.com/65porter" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Photography: Zach Pontz · Matt Fry / PSF project archives ↗</a></div></div><div class="office-reference"><a class="office-photo" href="assets/office-references/porter-hall.jpg" target="_blank" aria-label="View full photograph: 65 Porter working hall with pale timber room volumes beneath white exposed structure, occupied workstations and coloured details"><img src="assets/office-references/porter-hall.jpg" alt="65 Porter working hall with pale timber room volumes beneath white exposed structure, occupied workstations and coloured details" loading="lazy"><span>65 Porter ↗</span></a><div class="office-caption"><small>02 / Interior structure</small><h3 id="art/rooms-inside-a-larger-hall"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/rooms-inside-a-larger-hall">Rooms inside a larger hall</a></h3><p>Pale timber volumes stop below the white roof structure. Distinct objects carry the colour.</p><a class="office-credit" href="https://www.psfprojects.com/workplace/madwell-creative-agency" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Photography: Zach Pontz · Matt Fry / PSF project archives ↗</a></div></div><div class="office-reference"><a class="office-photo" href="assets/office-references/johnson-mezzanine.jpg" target="_blank" aria-label="View full photograph: 266 Johnson office with orange staircase and mezzanine railing, a tree under the skylight and a white Chrysler beside long desk rows"><img src="assets/office-references/johnson-mezzanine.jpg" alt="266 Johnson office with orange staircase and mezzanine railing, a tree under the skylight and a white Chrysler beside long desk rows" loading="lazy"><span>266 Johnson ↗</span></a><div class="office-caption"><small>04 / Landmark</small><h3 id="art/an-unmistakable-interior"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/an-unmistakable-interior">An unmistakable interior</a></h3><p>Orange stair and mezzanine, indoor tree, white Chrysler. Build the big recognisable forms first.</p><a class="office-credit" href="https://mattfryed.com/266-johnson" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Matt Fry portfolio · photographer not stated ↗</a></div></div></div><div class="office-boundary"><b>Boerum has its own street reference below.</b><p>The supplied street reference identifies its three adjoining brick-and-glass frontages. The city report’s uncaptioned interior still cannot establish a separate Boerum room or floor plan.</p><a href="research/office-assets/nyc-north-brooklyn-full-low.pdf#page=86" target="_blank">Read the city report · printed p.66 ↗</a></div><div class="figure-note">Matt’s portfolio credits his design and fabrication work at Porter and his interior design and construction oversight at Johnson. Porter separately credits PSF as architects. <a href="#research-offices/evidence-and-attribution">Source notes and attribution →</a></div></figure>

<p>The component system is accepted. The building forms, scene density and material palette remain in development. The current sites use Matt Fry’s documented office features; the earlier village architecture is archived. Place and department stay separate: Porter is a site, not an “Accounts building.”</p>
<figure class="plate art-plate" id="fig-A10"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">A10</span><h2 id="art/boerum-street-study"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/boerum-street-study">Boerum street study</a></h2></figcaption><div class="office-board"><div class="office-reference"><a class="office-photo" href="assets/office-references/boerum-user-street-view.png" target="_blank"><img src="assets/office-references/boerum-user-street-view.png" alt="User-identified Boerum offices: three adjoining brick and glass frontages with pale window frames and a tree beside the sidewalk" loading="lazy"><span>Boerum · supplied street view ↗</span></a><div class="office-caption"><h3 id="art/three-adjoining-frontages"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/three-adjoining-frontages">Three adjoining frontages</a></h3><p>Brick lower walls, large upper glazing grids, narrow entrances and a sidewalk tree.</p><small>User identification · image date unknown</small></div></div><div class="office-reference"><div class="office-canvas boerum-canvas"><canvas width="320" height="180" data-office-study="boerum" role="img" aria-label="Proposed pixel study of three connected Boerum frontages with a fedora-clad pedestrian pacing outside"></canvas></div><div class="office-caption"><div class="boerum-controls"><h3 id="art/the-frontage-in-motion"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/the-frontage-in-motion">The frontage in motion</a></h3><button type="button" id="boerum-motion" aria-pressed="true">Pause loop</button></div><p>Left, right, moonwalk left, right, left, moonwalk right. One 36-second loop along the three frontages.</p><small>Anonymous scene detail · proposed geometry</small></div></div></div><div class="figure-note">The three frontages are identified in the supplied reference. This is a facade and motion study, not a measured reconstruction. <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/previews/boerum-street-loop.gif" target="_blank">Street GIF ↗</a> · <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/previews/boerum-closeup-loop.gif" target="_blank">Walk-cycle GIF ↗</a> · <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#A-01" target="_blank">Animation frames ↗</a> <a href="#research-offices/boerum-street-reference">Reference and limits →</a></div></figure>

<p>The anonymous fedora walker repeats six traversals: left, right, moonwalk left, right, left, moonwalk right. Each traversal takes 5.4 seconds, followed by a 0.6-second pause. Both Boerum views use the same cycle; reduced motion and the pause control still apply.</p>
<figure class="plate art-plate" id="fig-A09"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">A09</span><h2 id="art/architecture-study"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/architecture-study">Architecture study</a></h2></figcaption><div class="office-study-heading"><strong>65 Porter · component translation</strong><span>PROPOSED GEOMETRY / SAME CAMERA</span></div><div class="office-proofs"><div><div class="office-canvas"><canvas width="320" height="180" data-office-study="shell" role="img" aria-label="Proposed pixel translation of Porter’s long industrial shell with tall windows, parapet and skylights"></canvas></div><h3 id="art/shell"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/shell">Shell</a></h3><p>Repeated window bays, shallow roof planes and grouped roof infrastructure.</p></div><div><div class="office-canvas"><canvas width="320" height="180" data-office-study="interior" role="img" aria-label="Same proposed Porter footprint with roof removed, timber room volumes and occupied workstations"></canvas></div><h3 id="art/roof-removed"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/roof-removed">Roof removed</a></h3><p>Timber rooms, white desk runs and gathering space retain the building’s identity.</p></div></div><div class="office-parts"><div class="office-canvas"><canvas width="320" height="180" data-office-study="parts" role="img" aria-label="Original industrial building components drawn at a shared pixel scale"></canvas></div><div><h3 id="art/parts-before-more-scenery"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/parts-before-more-scenery">Parts before more scenery</a></h3><p>Wall, glazing, skylight and interior insert use one projection. The exterior and interior belong to the same assembly.</p><p>This is a study of visible architectural features. Footprint, room positions and roof layout are proposed.</p></div></div><div class="office-material-strip"><div><i style="--a:#343a3a;--b:#52595a;--c:#777f7d"></i><b>Painted masonry</b></div><div><i style="--a:#9caaa9;--b:#ccd2cc;--c:#eceeea"></i><b>White structure</b></div><div><i style="--a:#92724c;--b:#c7a569;--c:#e4cd96"></i><b>Timber inserts</b></div><div><i style="--a:#8b372b;--b:#d9512d;--c:#f17a47"></i><b>Constructed colour</b></div></div><div class="figure-note">The approved component system remains. This study tests a new architectural vocabulary; the assembled park below now uses these office-derived forms. <a href="#research-offices/next-steps-spatial-and-recognition-tests">Next scene proof →</a></div></figure>

<p>Richness comes from repeated facade bays, layered interiors, visible work and distinctive gathering places. The assembled scene combines a compact street edge with planted courts. <strong>Next spatial proof:</strong> make one service route affect an ordinary handoff.</p>

<figure class="plate art-plate" id="fig-A03"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">A03</span><h2 id="art/scale-and-material"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/scale-and-material">Scale and material</a></h2></figcaption><div class="art-body"><div class="art-scale-study"><div class="art-native"><span>WORLD · SOURCE PIXELS</span><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="production" role="img" aria-label="Original production pixel specimen"></canvas><div class="art-scale-measures"><b>32 × 16</b><small>ground tile, source pixels</small><b>24 × 32</b><small>registered actor frame</small></div></div><div class="art-magnified"><span>WORLD · 2× DISPLAY</span><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="production" role="img" aria-label="Original production pixel specimen"></canvas><p>Integer geometry. Texture stays inside the material.</p></div><div class="art-type-sample"><span>INTERFACE · LIVE TEXT</span><b class="type-name">Geist Mono</b><p>Fabrication payment</p><strong>$12,000<span> USD</span></strong><div class="art-type-meta">O-19 / RECORD V2</div><small>Live amounts. sparse clustered texture.</p></div><div><div class="art-ramp"><i style="background:#202b31" title="#202b31"></i><i style="background:#343f43" title="#343f43"></i><i style="background:#55625f" title="#55625f"></i><i style="background:#8c9a91" title="#8c9a91"></i></div><b>Roof & shadow</b><p>Direction and plane before decoration.</p></div><div><div class="art-ramp"><i style="background:#858b8e" title="#858b8e"></i><i style="background:#a5afb0" title="#a5afb0"></i><i style="background:#c5cecb" title="#c5cecb"></i><i style="background:#e7e9dc" title="#e7e9dc"></i></div><b>Paving</b><p>Quieter than buildings and people.</p></div><div><div class="art-ramp"><i style="background:#683c36" title="#683c36"></i><i style="background:#9c5543" title="#9c5543"></i><i style="background:#c4825c" title="#c4825c"></i><i style="background:#e6b08a" title="#e6b08a"></i></div><b>Brick</b><p>Grouped courses, no photo texture.</p></div><div><div class="art-ramp"><i style="background:#207e52" title="#207e52"></i><i style="background:#23b766" title="#23b766"></i><i style="background:#4fea79" title="#4fea79"></i><i style="background:#abf6b7" title="#abf6b7"></i></div><b>Fresh ink</b><p>Selection, focus and active controls.</p></div><div><div class="art-ramp"><i style="background:#93496e" title="#93496e"></i><i style="background:#ca729e" title="#ca729e"></i><i style="background:#eda8c5" title="#eda8c5"></i><i style="background:#ffd2e5" title="#ffd2e5"></i></div><b>Carbon pink</b><p>Records and document furniture.</p></div></div></div><div class="figure-note">32×16 tiles and 24×32 actor frames are this study’s working scale, not a claim about original RCT dimensions. World texture has no evidentiary meaning.</div></figure>

<p>The world is drawn on an integer pixel grid. Text, controls and source documents remain independent of it. A building needs a recognisable roof, entrance and working interior before it needs surface detail.</p>
<figure class="plate art-plate" id="fig-A04"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">A04</span><h2 id="art/component-kit"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/component-kit">Component kit</a></h2></figcaption><div class="art-body"><div class="art-kit"><div class="art-kit-item"><div class="art-kit-top"><span>01</span><span>COMPONENT</span></div><div class="art-kit-image"><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="tile" role="img" aria-label="Original tile pixel specimen"></canvas></div><h4>Ground</h4><p>Grass, paved path, junction. and support details.</p></div><div class="art-kit-item"><div class="art-kit-top"><span>06</span><span>COMPONENT</span></div><div class="art-kit-image"><canvas width="160" height="116" specimen"></canvas></div><h4>Johnson workshop</h4><p>Yellow-brick loading frontage; orange stairs and a central white birch.</p></div></div></div><div class="figure-note">These are drawn components href="assets/sprite-catalogue/sheets/catalogue-overview.png" target="_blank">Full object sheet ↗</a></div></figure>

<p>The kit separates ground, props, people, signs and building parts. It can be extended without repainting a whole scene. Boerum, Porter and Johnson supply distinct site forms. Their placement together is a fictional park composition; no department, entity or historical chronology is inferred from a building style.</p>
<p>The <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">object catalogue</a> contains 44 registered objects and 121 transparent frames, with labelled overviews, per-object sheets and a frame manifest. The Boerum previews show the full 36-second cycle. Catalogue exports rebuild from the same render functions as the park. The full refinement redrew 43 earlier objects and added the complete Chrysler attraction assembly. The latest pass refines Supernova and corrects Johnson’s frontage and interior. Its car has 16 orientations. <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/sheets/refinement-comparison.png">Compare the previous and refined sprites</a>.</p>
<figure class="plate art-plate" id="fig-A05"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">A05</span><h2 id="art/assembly"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/assembly">Assembly</a></h2></figcaption><div class="art-body"><div class="art-construction"><div><span>01</span><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="stage0" role="img" aria-label="Original stage0 pixel specimen"></canvas><h4>Footprint</h4><p>Shared origin and floor plate.</p></div><div><span>02</span><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="stage1" role="img" aria-label="Original stage1 pixel specimen"></canvas><h4>Shell</h4><p>Two wall directions; fixed light.</p></div><div><span>03</span><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="stage2" role="img" aria-label="Original stage2 pixel specimen"></canvas><h4>Contents</h4><p>Furniture, worker and component.</p></div><div><span>04</span><canvas width="160" height="116" data-art-sprite="stage4" role="img" aria-label="Original stage4 pixel specimen"></canvas><h4>Roof</h4><p>A separate removable object.</p></div></div><div class="art-assembly-controls" role="group" aria-label="Show campus assembly layer"><button data-art-layer="0" aria-pressed="false">1. Select a landmark for its sprite and source photograph. <button class="art-expand" data-art-expand>Expand scene ↗</button></div></figure>

<p>Sites and scenery can be selected separately. A landmark opens its photograph and identifies the invented park behaviour. Courtyard choices change the visible installation; the Chrysler can remain parked or run a miniature circuit. These are art-study interactions, not a working economy.</p>
<p>Each building has visible <strong>Exterior / Interior</strong> previews. The inspector consistently shows the selected sprite, source photograph and park interpretation. The <strong>Practice documents</strong> tray opens C-07 independently of building selection and returns to the same park state. The proposed Work, Cash, Information, Control and People views remain in the <a href="#interface/five-views">interface study</a>; architectural browsing does not use those tabs.</p>
<p>The arrangement combines architecture and objects from different sites and photographs. It is not a historical map or a claim that the event objects were permanent. The next play test should make one spatial choice affect an ordinary production handoff before adding more attractions.</p>

Selection remains attached.</p></div></div><details class="dm-reference-drawer"><summary>Material references / supplied images</summary><div class="dm-reference-grid"><a href="assets/ui-references/receipt-type.png" target="_blank"><img src="assets/ui-references/receipt-type.png" alt="Receipt / type reference" loading="lazy"><span>Receipt / type reference ↗</span></a><a href="assets/ui-references/ascii-files.png" target="_blank"><img src="assets/ui-references/ascii-files.png" alt="Character-built files" loading="lazy"><span>Character-built files ↗</span></a><a href="assets/ui-references/workstation.png" target="_blank"><img src="assets/ui-references/workstation.png" alt="Monochrome workstation" loading="lazy"><span>Monochrome workstation ↗</span></a><a href="assets/ui-references/form-type.png" target="_blank"><img src="assets/ui-references/form-type.png" alt="Ruled type specimen" loading="lazy"><span>Ruled type specimen ↗</span></a></div><p>Visual references, not assets to copy. Geist and Geist Mono are used here; the typefaces shown in the references have not been installed.</p></details></div><div class="figure-note">Switch documents, compare saved versions, pin the bank strip or edit the note. The tax-form study is separate from C-07. <a href="#art/world-and-interface">Try the same materials in the park inspector ↑</a></div></figure>

<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Material</th>
<th>Information it carries</th>
<th>Interaction</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Ruled invoice</td>
<td>Supplier, obligation, amount and terms</td>
<td>Open the obligation; pin its related bank extract</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Carbon copy</td>
<td>Platform label, saved version, effective and recorded times</td>
<td>Switch v1/v2 without overwriting either snapshot</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Perforated strip</td>
<td>Dated bank movements and the extract’s limits</td>
<td>Pin alongside the invoice or saved record</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Numbered form</td>
<td>Entity, period, revision and fields</td>
<td>Future binding opens the original page and line; the current specimen is unbound</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Ruled note</td>
<td>Player interpretation and linked record IDs</td>
<td>Edit a question; preserve it while switching documents</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>File tabs and terminal index</td>
<td>Record identity and relationships</td>
<td>Change document while retaining the selected object</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>RCT’s object inspector remains the organising model: select, inspect, compare, return. One reader with one pinned comparison is enough for this proof. Documents have fixed reading positions; managing a pile of draggable windows is not the task.</p>
<p>The invoice and bank extract are not interchangeable receipts. A platform label stays inside its dated record. The source image, when bound, retains its original appearance in a separate viewer; themed summaries never replace native evidence.</p>
<p>Geist remains the navigation face. Geist Mono handles IDs, narrow records and tabular figures. Sprocket holes, perforations and dense dot patterns stay at the edge of the reading surface. No texture, random ink loss or simulated screen flicker crosses a number. Pink identifies a record copy, green identifies an active control; neither is a verdict.</p>
<p>The populated specimens use the fictional C-07 / O-19 / P-04 fixture. The added v1 label and its times are invented interaction values. T-01 is a separate, unbound form-layout study, not an IRS form or a record connected to C-07. The 2024 IRS Form 941-X is linked only as a visual reference for field and part structure. Player notes persist across document changes but reset on page reload.</p>
<h2 id="art/production-and-next-proof"><a class="section-anchor" href="#art/production-and-next-proof">Production and next proof</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Built in this study</th>
<th>Next production proof</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Original raster primitives, one projection, named material ramps</td>
<td>Compare a model-to-sprite export against this kit at the same size</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Three office-derived buildings with removable roofs, mixed occupancy and shared furnishings</td>
<td>Walk a person behind a wall, through a doorway and past furniture without overlap errors</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Ground, paths, landscape, signage, props and people reused in the scene</td>
<td>Add twenty objects without changing camera, light or pixel scale</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Five inspector views preserve the selected building</td>
<td>Test whether readers distinguish place, obligation, attempt and record</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Separate readable record pane and bank-event strip</td>
<td>Bind one historical scene to inspected native exhibits</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Object catalogue, individual PNG/GIF exports, atlases, anchors and orientation metadata</td>
<td>Add occlusion masks and approve a complete production animation set</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>This pass demonstrates a visual system and its interactions. It does not establish a complete animation set, a running economy or an approved production engine. The current procedural drawings are an editable art proof; modelled source assets can replace them only if they preserve the resulting image.</p>
<p>Sound follows ordinary work: footsteps, fabrication, paper handling and deliveries. Reading pauses ambient motion. A future sound pass should test one handoff, one waiting state and one record opening; the same states must remain clear with sound off.</p>
<p>Source images and third-party game art stay in the reference board. Supplied PZ mods remain craft references with their stated reuse restrictions. The <a href="https://github.com/OpenRCT2/Blender-RCT-Graphics" target="_blank" rel="noopener">OpenRCT2 graphics helper</a> documents a historical sprite workflow; its Blender 2.79 dependency makes it a reference, not the proposed production setup. The <a href="https://steamcommunity.com/workshop/filedetails/?id=2337452747" target="_blank" rel="noopener">supplied tile mod</a> is likewise a construction reference, not an asset license for this game.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#interface"><small>Previous</small>Interface</a><a href="#production"><small>Next</small>Build &amp; test →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-production" hidden aria-labelledby="title-production"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">CHAPTER / 07</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-production" tabindex="-1">Build &amp; test</h1></header><div class="chapter-intro"><p>Choose the campaign and engine after one complete episode has passed source and play tests.</p>
</div><nav class="page-links" aria-label="In Build &amp; test"><a href="#production/architecture"><span>01</span>Architecture</a><a href="#production/mod-boundary"><span>02</span>Mod boundary</a><a href="#production/source-review"><span>03</span>Source review</a><a href="#production/production-sequence"><span>04</span>Production sequence</a><a href="#production/responsibilities"><span>05</span>Responsibilities</a><a href="#production/acceptance-tests"><span>06</span>Acceptance tests</a><a href="#production/open-decisions-and-next-steps"><span>07</span>Open decisions and next steps</a><a href="#production/change-record"><span>08</span>Change record</a></nav><div class="release-note">DESIGN 0.10 / ART 0.9<br><a href="previews/index.html">Current previews ↗</a><br><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">Object catalogue ↗</a><br><a href="archive/index.html">Archive ↗</a></div></aside><div class="chapter-main"><article class="article-body"><figure class="plate editorial-plate" id="fig-D05"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">D05</span><strong>Current system</strong></figcaption><div class="deliverable-grid"><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html"><img src="assets/sprite-catalogue/sheets/buildings-overview.png" alt="Three building families from the current 44-object sprite library" class="pixel-preview" loading="lazy"><b>Object library</b><span>44 objects · 121 frame slots<br>PNG, GIF, sheets and manifest ↗</span></a><a href="previews/index.html"><img src="assets/art-system/campus-cutaway.png" alt="Current scene with the selected roof removed" class="pixel-preview" loading="lazy"><b>Preview library</b><span>Current scenes, components and motion<br>One place to inspect and download ↗</span></a><a href="#binding"><img src="assets/zero-machine-page-34.png" alt="Original Creative Peaks page in The Zero Machine" class="" loading="lazy"><b>Source binding</b><span>Export fields inspected<br>Historical interaction inactive →</span></a></div><div class="figure-note">The reader, previews and catalogue share the current renderers and source manifest. Superseded studies belong in the <a href="archive/index.html">versioned archive ↗</a>.</div></figure>

<figure class="plate" id="fig-10"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">FIG. 10</span><h2 id="production/architecture"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/architecture">Architecture</a></h2></figcaption><div class="figure-body"><div class="architecture"><div class="architecture-top"><div><b>Source archive + claims</b><span>Inputs, locators, corrections, review states</span></div><div><b>Historical objects + events</b><span>Identities, versions, relationships, uncertainty</span></div><div class="fiction-boundary"><b>Scenario model</b><span>Invented parameters, actions, seeded events</span></div></div><div class="architecture-bottom"><div><b>Presentation</b><span>World · views · records · accessible lists</span></div><div><b>Review tools</b><span>Coverage · change impact · source-to-scene links</span></div></div></div></div><div class="figure-note">The scenario has its own state. It cannot overwrite source records or the historical model.</div></figure>

<p>The first prototype can use a small canvas/SVG scene with a DOM interface. Engine selection follows measured scene complexity and art needs.</p>
<p>An event has an economic identity and a representation identity. Returns reference an attempt; reissues reference an obligation; edits reference prior versions. Use typed, sourced many-to-many links for batch members, partial settlements, repeated attempts and multi-client records. Shared amount/date or journal context does not establish identity. Do not invent fractional client allocations for unsplit objects.</p>
<p>Keep effective time and recorded time. A source correction visibly invalidates dependent narrative and figures. Saves include scenario version, source/data version, seed, decisions, pins, layout and view state. Fictional parameters never write to historical data. Exports state their population and basis.</p>
<h2 id="production/mod-boundary"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/mod-boundary">Mod boundary</a></h2>
<p>Separate art packs, fictional scenarios and versioned source/document packs. A manifest declares model compatibility, dependencies, license and historical or invented status. Use separate save namespaces and retain provenance. JSON scenarios and replaceable atlases may suffice; Workshop integration and unrestricted scripting are not prototype commitments.</p>
<h2 id="production/source-review"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/source-review">Source review</a></h2>
<p>A complete historical-scene record needs: proposition; event and record dates; source and locator; amount, currency and population; supporting and conflicting evidence; endpoint boundary; source assessment; inspection status; withdrawn predecessors; allowed player action; simulated assumptions; and the argument the mechanic teaches.</p>
<p>Check both arithmetic and meaning: denominator, identity, period, population, quotation context and endpoint. The current <a href="#evidence">evidence map</a> records unresolved routes and discrepancies; this design pass does not certify the case.</p>
<p>Each historical scene needs source/financial, gameplay-comprehension and interface-semantics review. AI review is preparation for the relevant human/source authority. Record disagreements beside the claim and hold the affected historical interaction until resolved.</p>
<figure class="plate" id="fig-11"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">FIG. 11</span><h2 id="production/production-sequence"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/production-sequence">Production sequence</a></h2></figcaption><div class="figure-body"><ol class="gates"><li><span class="gate-number">1</span><div><h4>Bind sources</h4><p>Inspect one complete episode and a legitimate control.</p></div><span class="parallel">Alongside: synthetic capacity + art study</span></li><li><span class="gate-number">2</span><div><h4>Agree on objects</h4><p>Follow the same job through all five views.</p></div><span class="parallel">Alongside: player-role storyboards</span></li><li><span class="gate-number">3</span><div><h4>Compare structures</h4><p>Test operating and reconstruction versions.</p></div><span class="parallel">Alongside: typography + scene readability</span></li><li><span class="gate-number">4</span><div><h4>Build one episode</h4><p>12–15 minutes; one conflict and one valid control.</p></div><span class="parallel">Alongside: next chapter’s source work</span></li><li><span class="gate-number">5</span><div><h4>Lock the campaign</h4><p>Sequence approved mechanisms and historical anchors.</p></div><span class="parallel">Then: assets, authoring and production</span></li></ol></div></figure>

<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Phase</th>
<th>Work and concrete output</th>
<th>Proceed only when</th>
<th>Work that can run alongside it</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>0. Source orientation — complete at stated scope</td>
<td>Corpus inventory, full chapter coverage, 48-technique routing, corrections, draft GDD, research and style options</td>
<td>Assumptions, corrections and inspection limits are visible</td>
<td>Reference research and art refinement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1. Source binding — active</td>
<td>First episode packet, exact export locators and conflicts recorded; retrieve originals and close the identity links</td>
<td>No unresolved numeric/identity claim is needed by that episode</td>
<td>Synthetic capacity prototype; sprite readability study</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2. Ontology workshop</td>
<td>Map a contract, site, entity, employee, supplier, obligation, payment and record through one complete job</td>
<td>Same object can survive all views without changing meaning</td>
<td>Two competing player-role storyboards</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3. Paper/clickable playtest</td>
<td>Test operating-campus and reconstruction-park versions of one episode</td>
<td>Players can choose and explain consequences; ordinary play has appeal</td>
<td>Art style tiles and document legibility tests</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4. Thin playable slice</td>
<td>12–15 minute loop, limited placeable objects, one conflict and one valid control</td>
<td>No systematic confusion about money/state/provenance; players voluntarily try a second plan</td>
<td>Source work on next chapter; pipeline cost benchmark</td>
</tr>
<tr>

<td>Sequence learning prerequisites, historical anchors, permissions, events and ending</td>
<td>Each chapter earns a new distinction and has source coverage</td>
<td>Additional art and sound studies only for approved objects</td>
</tr>

<td>6. Production</td>
<td>Author episodes, assets and software against the chosen model</td>

<td>Accessibility, saves, performance, mod boundary</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>7. Release preparation</td>
<td>Audience-specific editing, attribution, rights, packaging and distribution checks</td>
<td>Known review findings resolved; version and source scope explicit</td>
<td>Optional read-only infographic export</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Estimate after the first source-bound slice using verified scenes, unique transitions, assets and review effort. The located validation package is an archival collection; full campaign coverage has not been assessed.</p>
<h2 id="production/responsibilities"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/responsibilities">Responsibilities</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Role</th>
<th>Owns</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Source editor</td>
<td>Claims, corrections, chronology and coverage</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Systems designer</td>
<td>Actions, state transitions and incentives</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>UX designer</td>
<td>Continuity, disclosure, comparison and accessible paths</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Art / technical art</td>
<td>Camera, scale, asset grammar and readable states</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Developer</td>
<td>Model, renderer, persistence and replay tools</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Integrator</td>
<td>Agreement across the model, narrative, figures and software</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Workshop one scene through all roles: define its distinction, propose two decisions, draw the path, identify misleading marks, estimate hidden state and test a legitimate counterexample.</p>
<h2 id="production/acceptance-tests"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/acceptance-tests">Acceptance tests</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Test</th>
<th>Prompt / setup</th>
<th>Failure means</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Ordinary play</td>
<td>Three minutes of production with no anomaly and two viable plans</td>
<td>If boring, revise management before adding investigation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>State discrimination</td>
<td>Paid/settled, returned/Paid, valid pooling, duplicate records, unknown endpoint</td>
<td>Any recurring false inference stops content expansion</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>View continuity</td>
<td>Follow one obligation through five views and return</td>
<td>Lost identity/time requires interface redesign</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission model</td>
<td>Owner lacks export; operator can initiate but not approve</td>
<td>If title substitutes for authority, model or UI is wrong</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Nonadditivity</td>
<td>Show bill, batch member, ledger and bank event</td>
<td>Counting one episode twice is a release blocker</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>History versus fiction</td>
<td>Ask what the player changed and what actually happened</td>
<td>Blurred answers require stronger separation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Recall</td>
<td>Next day: sketch work→obligation→attempt→settlement→record</td>
<td>Memorized accusations without usable distinctions is failure</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Art readability</td>
<td>Identify roles, selection and blocked work at two zooms</td>
<td>If style destroys legibility, adjust assets before enlarging world</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Artifact QA</td>
<td>Keyboard, scaling, narrow layout, reduced motion, save/reopen</td>
<td>Navigation and reading must remain reliable</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Start with six to eight participants with different management-game and financial familiarity. These are diagnostic sessions. Record behavior and mistaken explanations; avoid instructions that teach the answer immediately before testing it.</p>
<h2 id="production/open-decisions-and-next-steps"><a class="section-anchor" href="#production/open-decisions-and-next-steps">Open decisions and next steps</a></h2>
<ol>
<li>First audience and the understanding they should leave with.</li>
<li>Whether agency means fictional operations, historical discovery or explicit alternative history.</li>
<li>Obtain the current source-release manifest and the original statements and identity links listed in the binding packet.</li>
<li>Three objects whose ordinary operation should be enjoyable.</li>
<li>Legitimate transaction paired with the first adverse example.</li>
<li>Refine building aesthetics within the accepted pixel system; test recognition at native size.</li>
</ol>
<p>Audience and player authority remain open design decisions. Binding now uses CASE-01 as the narrative reference and the located validation package as an archival source collection; neither overrides conflicting records. Synthetic interaction and office-art studies can continue alongside that review.</p>

</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#art"><small>Previous</small>Art &amp; sound</a><a href="#evidence"><small>Next</small>Evidence →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-evidence" hidden aria-labelledby="title-evidence"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">CHAPTER / 08</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-evidence" tabindex="-1">Evidence</h1></header><div class="source-preface"><p>Reviewed for edition 0.10 · 16 September 2026 · source scope below</p>
</div><nav class="page-links" aria-label="In Evidence"><a href="#evidence/what-has-actually-been-read"><span>01</span>What has actually been read</a><a href="#evidence/first-binding-pass"><span>02</span>First binding pass</a><a href="#evidence/source-classes"><span>03</span>Source classes</a><a href="#evidence/the-story-in-seven-movements"><span>04</span>The story in seven movements</a><a href="#evidence/chapter-coverage-of-the-whole-pdf"><span>05</span>Chapter coverage of the whole PDF</a><a href="#evidence/cast-organizations-and-authority-over-time"><span>06</span>Cast, organizations and authority over time</a><a href="#evidence/non-negotiable-distinctions-in-the-model"><span>07</span>Non-negotiable distinctions in the model</a><a href="#evidence/issues-found-during-this-pass"><span>08</span>Issues found during this pass</a><a href="#evidence/all-48-source-techniques-design-coverage"><span>09</span>All 48 source techniques: design coverage</a><a href="#evidence/claim-record-required-before-historical-content-ships"><span>10</span>Claim record required before historical content ships</a></nav><a class="source-document source-document-large" href="../The_Zero_Machine_Specific_Story_Reconstruction%20FINAL.pdf#page=1" target="_blank" aria-label="Open The Zero Machine PDF, 58 pages"><span class="source-page"><img src="assets/zero-machine-page-01.png" alt="Actual first page of The Zero Machine, September 2, 2026" loading="lazy"></span><span class="source-description"><span class="source-code">CASE-01 · 58 PAGES</span><strong>The Zero Machine</strong><span>Specific story reconstruction</span><span class="source-open">Open document ↗</span></span></a><div class="document-tools"><a href="evidence-map.md">Editable source ↗</a></div></aside><div class="chapter-main"><article class="article-body"><h2 id="evidence/what-has-actually-been-read"><a class="section-anchor" href="#evidence/what-has-actually-been-read">What has actually been read</a></h2>
<p>The complete 58-page text of <strong>The Zero Machine — Specific Story Reconstruction FINAL.pdf</strong>, including its appendices, was read for this pass. Selected rendered pages were inspected to check tables and visual context. This began as a review of a reconstruction. The first binding pass now also inspects selected bank-data, BILL and QBO exports; original bank statements, invoice originals, full audit history and recipient confirmations have not yet been verified. Tax, correspondence and court records remain outside that bounded inspection. The word “proved” inside the PDF describes the reconstruction&#39;s assessment; it does not describe verification performed in this design pass.</p>
<p>The PDF&#39;s opening date is September 2, 2026. Its SHA-256 is <code>9352893711180900fcd770651011c65036504a66570490138d68fcd9a5c253e8</code>. That hash identifies this exact input. “FINAL” in a filename does not establish authority over unavailable exhibits. It remains the narrative reference for binding; a conflicting underlying record is preserved beside its claim.</p>
<p>The review also inspected the old explainer&#39;s content map, handoff, verification report, and selected implementation/data references; the two visual references; the supplied screenshot; the garden source and rendered page; and selected technique-library records and the HOLY_OPS GDD&#39;s section structure. No old verification suite was rerun against a missing corpus. Its recorded PASS is historical evidence of a check, not a present certificate.</p>
<figure class="plate editorial-plate" id="fig-D06"><figcaption><span class="figure-id">D06</span><strong>Keep the source and the review separate</strong></figcaption><div class="evidence-example"><a href="../The_Zero_Machine_Specific_Story_Reconstruction%20FINAL.pdf#page=34"><img src="assets/zero-machine-page-34.png" alt="Unaltered source page 34 of The Zero Machine" class="" loading="lazy"><span>CASE-01 · page 34 ↗</span></a><div><div class="evidence-layer"><small>SOURCE CLAIM</small><b>Return with Paid retained</b><span>The reconstruction’s account, with its page locator.</span></div><div class="evidence-layer"><small>INSPECTED MATERIAL</small><b>Selected export fields</b><span>BILL snapshots, bank extractions and QBO entries. Exact file hashes and row locators are retained.</span></div><div class="evidence-layer"><small>UNRESOLVED</small><b>Originals, links and endpoint</b><span>Return identity, October 23 bank direction, continuous status history and final recipient settlement remain open.</span></div><a href="#binding-inventory">Read the source inventory →</a></div></div><div class="figure-note">A source’s conclusion and the project’s inspection status answer different questions. Publishing this design review does not activate historical gameplay.</div></figure>

<h2 id="evidence/first-binding-pass"><a class="section-anchor" href="#evidence/first-binding-pass">First binding pass</a></h2>
<p>The <a href="#binding">Creative Peaks packet</a> and <a href="#binding-inventory">source inventory</a> record exact export rows, file hashes, unresolved joins and exclusions. They narrow the source scope; they do not certify the full validation package.</p>

<h2 id="evidence/the-story-in-seven-movements"><a class="section-anchor" href="#evidence/the-story-in-seven-movements">The story in seven movements</a></h2>
<p>Every sentence in this table is a summary of CASE-01, not a separately verified finding. Names identify source subjects, not preassigned game villains.</p>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Movement</th>
<th>Source</th>
<th>What changes</th>
<th>What a player must already understand</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>A real business grows</td>
<td>pp.1–6, 10; N pp.56–57</td>
<td>Creative and production work creates client obligations, revenue, costs and timing pressure</td>
<td>A budget is not cash received; production is not automatically outside pass-through</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Responsibility and authority separate</td>
<td>pp.10–16</td>
<td>Rogers builds linked operating records; the account describes decisive gates remaining elsewhere and her function transferring to Tucker</td>
<td>An org title does not give every permission</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A reporting preference becomes infrastructure</td>
<td>pp.16–20</td>
<td>Pooled payments, hosted books, paired revenue/cost entries and recurring processing become routine</td>
<td>A clearing account is a record mechanism, not a bank</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Different audiences see different companies</td>
<td>pp.21–28</td>
<td>Hong Kong reporting, owner routes, relief and lender presentations develop alongside real work</td>
<td>Work, cash, reported performance and entitlement differ</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The return path matters</td>
<td>pp.23–26, 30–34</td>
<td>Returned transfers, failed wires, duplicate payment stories and conflicting settlement states</td>
<td>Gross movement is not retained benefit; identical amounts do not prove identity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Ownership transfers while control lags</td>
<td>pp.29–38</td>
<td>The account connects migration, tax preparation, financing, settlement and incomplete access</td>
<td>Legal ownership, account administration and payment authority are distinct</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The company closes; the records continue</td>
<td>pp.39–40, 45–47</td>
<td>Platform accounts continue actions after operations cease; further records are sought</td>
<td>A named account is not proof of the person using it; investigation is not adjudication</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>The likely opening is ordinary production work. Beginning with the largest aggregate or an accusation gives players the answer before they understand the nouns.</p>
