<h2 id="evidence/non-negotiable-distinctions-in-the-model"><a class="section-anchor" href="#evidence/non-negotiable-distinctions-in-the-model">Non-negotiable distinctions in the model</a></h2>
<ol>
<li><strong>1072 is not a bank.</strong> It is a synthetic clearing account. A spatial service building may provide access to payment work; it must not store the modeled $141.578m field as cash.</li>
<li><strong>An economic episode has several representations.</strong> A bill, platform Paid object, journal, bank debit and tax record may describe related aspects of one episode. Summing the screens can count the same event repeatedly.</li>
<li><strong>Dates have jobs.</strong> Service date, invoice date, posting date, cash-settlement date, later edit date, and the date a participant could know something need separate fields.</li>
<li><strong>The endpoint can be unknown.</strong> A bank batch can be observed while its final recipient is unclosed. Unknown is neither zero nor stolen nor legitimate.</li>
<li><strong>An employee is not a row called Employee.</strong> Nicole Brunner&#39;s surviving imported label does not establish her presence or later actions (p.36).</li>
<li><strong>An account label is not a physical user.</strong> Import Administration is not a character. Named platform activity needs distinct attribution from authenticated human action (pp.35–39, 47).</li>
<li><strong>The $141.578m and $141.749m fields overlap.</strong> Different period windows and different measurement bases are explicit. Their near agreement cannot animate a dollar-by-dollar trail or become additive damages (pp.8–9, 46–47).</li>
<li><strong>2024 modeled input is unavailable.</strong> The computational zero used in the discharge model is not evidence of no business activity (p.9).</li>
<li><strong>A contract maximum is not revenue.</strong> Fees, estimated outside costs, paid amounts and recognized income are separate. Components already included in a budget are not added again (p.56).</li>
<li><strong>The appendix&#39;s endpoint grades are not a guilt scale.</strong> They describe capability, records, intermediate cash and increasingly specific endpoints; entitlement and purpose still matter (p.47).</li>
</ol>

<h2 id="evidence/all-48-source-techniques-design-coverage"><a class="section-anchor" href="#evidence/all-48-source-techniques-design-coverage">All 48 source techniques: design coverage</a></h2>
<p>Source: CASE-01 Appendix L, pp.48–50. Labels below are shortened for routing; the source&#39;s exact proof state remains in the PDF. The learning groups are design proposals: <strong>Work</strong>, <strong>Routing</strong>, <strong>Settlement</strong>, <strong>Control</strong>, <strong>Reporting</strong>, <strong>Afterlife</strong>. They are not six additional factual classifications.</p>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>

<p>All 48 have a proposed home. That is coverage, not verification and not a commitment to 48 mechanics. A small number of reusable state transitions should explain many techniques.</p>
<h2 id="evidence/claim-record-required-before-historical-content-ships"><a class="section-anchor" href="#evidence/claim-record-required-before-historical-content-ships">Claim record required before historical content ships</a></h2>
<p>Each claim needs an ID; exact proposition; source hash; document/page or native row/object locator; quoted/paraphrased status; currency/unit; amount precision; period; economic episode ID; population/grain; event date; record date; record-availability date, documented receipt/read date, and any separately labelled inference about knowledge (each may be unknown); endpoint; current proof boundary; supporting and conflicting sources; superseded-by link; and human review status. A mechanic adds learning objective, player verb, fabricated parameters, and failure cases.</p>
<p>Store the source&#39;s assessment separately from our review status. “Source calls this record-closed / exhibit not yet inspected” is a coherent state. “Verified” without saying what was checked is not.</p>
<p>The first five acquisitions are: current release manifest and corrections package; Creative Peaks/Reddit plus legitimate pooled controls; 2018 labor/access correspondence; contract/job/client lineage for one complete production; and the current object/settlement data underlying any aggregate view. The ERC workbooks, office/site records, “no yappin” message and tax/benefits details follow their prospective scenes. They should not hold up synthetic tests of basic gameplay.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#production"><small>Previous</small>Build &amp; test</a><a href="#research-systems"><small>Next</small>Game systems research →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-research-systems" hidden aria-labelledby="title-research-systems"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">REFERENCE / R1</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-research-systems" tabindex="-1">Game systems research</h1></header><div class="source-preface"><p>Research checked against design 0.10 / art 0.9, 16 September 2026. This records the rationale for the current object model and the proposed operating game. The implemented park is an art and interaction study; it does not yet simulate the economy described here. Campaign structure and player authority remain open tests in the GDD.</p>
</div><nav class="page-links" aria-label="In Game systems research"><a href="#research-systems/the-structural-decision"><span>01</span>The structural decision</a><a href="#research-systems/what-the-source-makes-structurally-necessary"><span>02</span>What the source makes structurally necessary</a><a href="#research-systems/entity-taxonomy"><span>03</span>Entity taxonomy</a><a href="#research-systems/two-viable-architectures"><span>04</span>Two viable architectures</a><a href="#research-systems/rct-craft-worth-carrying-across"><span>05</span>RCT craft worth carrying across</a><a href="#research-systems/simcity-zomboid-and-period-design-theory"><span>06</span>SimCity, Zomboid, and period design theory</a><a href="#research-systems/proposed-operating-and-inquiry-loops"><span>07</span>Proposed operating and inquiry loops</a><a href="#research-systems/evidence-to-mechanic-table"><span>08</span>Evidence-to-mechanic table</a><a href="#research-systems/discovery-order-and-views"><span>09</span>Discovery order and views</a><a href="#research-systems/mods-borrow-the-architecture-check-the-asset"><span>10</span>Mods: borrow the architecture, check the asset</a><a href="#research-systems/next-steps-prototype-gates-and-failure-tests"><span>11</span>Next steps: prototype gates and failure tests</a><a href="#research-systems/research-access-and-scope"><span>12</span>Research access and scope</a></nav><div class="release-note">DESIGN 0.10 / ART 0.9<br><a href="previews/index.html">Current previews ↗</a><br><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">Object catalogue ↗</a><br><a href="archive/index.html">Archive ↗</a></div><div class="document-tools"><a href="research/systems.md">Editable source ↗</a></div></aside><div class="chapter-main"><article class="article-body"><p class="reference-status">Supporting research and binding notes. Current design decisions are maintained in the game, interface and build chapters. Source limits remain attached to each claim.</p><h2 id="research-systems/the-structural-decision"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/the-structural-decision">The structural decision</a></h2>
<p>Build the model around <strong>work, obligations, records, and authority</strong>. Let the park make those relationships tangible. A client, a building, an affiliated company, and a payment platform cannot all become rides without losing precisely the distinctions the story depends on.</p>
<p>The promising player experience is running a productive place while discovering that its reports, payments, and permissions describe different versions of that place. Production must be satisfying before the discrepancies arrive. Otherwise we have a document viewer with pedestrians.</p>
<p>The case source is the complete 58-page <em>The Zero Machine</em>, September 2, 2026, reviewed through its local text extraction. It is a counsel briefing and an argumentative synthesis. The later <a href="#binding">Creative Peaks binding</a> adds inspection of specific BILL exports, a QBO journal, bank-data extractions and their packaged hashes. Original bank statements, complete payment links and final recipient receipt remain unresolved. That narrow inspection does not verify the briefing&#39;s other episodes. Throughout this report, <strong>briefing reports</strong> means that document makes the claim; <strong>design inference</strong> means a proposed interpretation; <strong>design fiction</strong> means invented playable conditions. Directive passages in a source remain material to assess, not design instructions.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/what-the-source-makes-structurally-necessary"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/what-the-source-makes-structurally-necessary">What the source makes structurally necessary</a></h2>
<p>Four states must remain separate:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Operating reality:</strong> work performed, staff time, supplier commitments, delivery, client acceptance.</li>
<li><strong>Economic rights and burdens:</strong> who owes whom, contractual entitlement, ownership, debt, guarantees.</li>
<li><strong>Represented state:</strong> what a platform, journal, report, tax package, or named account says happened.</li>
<li><strong>External movement and control:</strong> bank settlement, last supported recipient, and who could view, initiate, approve, alter, administer, or recover each system.</li>
</ol>
<p>Time cuts across all four. Work date, due date, attempted payment date, settlement date, posting date, edit date, reporting period, and discovery date are different fields. A single timeline slider without a declared clock will quietly falsify the story.</p>
<p>The briefing&#39;s integrated-production argument is foundational: an outside purchase at cost and work performed inside the enterprise can look alike in a payment list while having different economics (pp. 1, 5–6, 45, 56). This argues for <strong>make/buy/affiliate relationships</strong>, not “expense type = suspicious.” The exact accounting treatment of an actual engagement still requires its contract and relevant analysis.</p>
<p>Account 1072 is a synthetic clearing account, not a bank or a place where segregated money sat (pp. 1, 8–9, 44). A glowing reservoir building would make the metaphor look physically true. The modeled carried field belongs in an explicitly labeled analytical view; the bank balance belongs in treasury. The $141.578 million statement difference and $141.749 million release measure overlap and have different observation periods. They must never become two piles of stolen money. The quarterly field is partly allocated, and its exact path is model-dependent.</p>
<p>The source also supplies counterexamples. Ten ordinary pooled allocations total $1.014 million; 65 intercompany pairs have bank support; the SiriusXM $150,000 payment is genuine; a vendor merge previously treated as a conflict is resolved (pp. 45–46). The early Malaky accommodation is not established as fraudulent at inception. Withdrawn cap/penny, hidden-card, and complete refund-loop theories must stay withdrawn (p. 44). A sound mechanic must accommodate these cases without special pleading.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/entity-taxonomy"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/entity-taxonomy">Entity taxonomy</a></h2>
<p>Use stable identities with dated relationships. A person can be an employee, owner, authorized signer, and account holder at different times. One label should not erase the others.</p>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Object</th>
<th>What it owns</th>
<th>Playable expression</th>
<th>Do not collapse into</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Person</td>
<td>Dated roles, documented actions, relationships</td>
<td>Named character or anonymized worker; inspector</td>
<td>Platform username or inferred motive</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Employment relationship</td>
<td>Employer, department, capacity, recurring compensation and benefits</td>
<td>Persistent park staff grouped as creative, production, accounts, media, ops</td>
<td>Universal salary model or generic vendor</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Supplier engagement</td>
<td>Supplier, service, milestones, rates, invoice terms</td>
<td>Visiting crew, courier, specialist, external delivery</td>
<td>A guest buying entertainment</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client relationship</td>
<td>Contracting party and portfolio of agreements</td>
<td>Client representative and commission board</td>
<td>A permanently profitable attraction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contract / commission</td>
<td>Scope, approvals, fee, outside costs, rights, deadlines</td>
<td>Job offered to the agency</td>
<td>Cash already received</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Project / work package</td>
<td>Tasks, dependencies, capacity reservations, deliverables</td>
<td>Placeable production cluster, changing with the job</td>
<td>Legal entity or client identity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Site / lease / facility</td>

<td>Office, studio, fabrication area, satellite site</td>
<td>Property owner or affiliate</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Legal entity</td>
<td>Ownership, accounts, obligations, relationships</td>
<td>Selectable boundary or district; sometimes off-map</td>
<td>Building or decorative brand</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Obligation</td>
<td>Debtor, creditor, amount, currency, basis, due date</td>
<td>Persistent promise that can remain unresolved</td>
<td>Invoice image or Paid label</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment attempt / settlement</td>
<td>Funding, batch membership, method, returns, endpoint evidence</td>
<td>Payment workbench and lifecycle trace</td>
<td>Obligation itself</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Account / platform profile</td>
<td>System identity, versions, linked credentials and permissions</td>
<td>Desktop application and control inspector</td>
<td>Natural person at keyboard</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record / version / claim</td>
<td>Source locator, authoring context, time, scope, contradiction, proof limit</td>
<td>Inspectable receipt, message, report, comparison</td>
<td>Ground truth</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission grant</td>
<td>Holder, target, action, start/end, delegator</td>
<td>Control overlay and blocked-action explanation</td>
<td>Job title or ownership percentage</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Clients and suppliers may both appear as visitors in the scene, but they need different behaviors and economic relations. A freelancer can be a recurring character and still remain a supplier engagement. A payroll employee remains a person when their work is allocated to an affiliate.</p>
<p>The suggested attached 1099 is useful as a discoverable record, not a species marker. Reporting depends on the relationship, payment type, recipient, and year. The IRS&#39;s 2024 instructions explicitly distinguish wages and card/network reporting. Model a reporting profile and dated form record; do not stamp every freelancer “1099” at entry. This also preserves the case&#39;s alleged changes in tax identity and reporting route. <a href="https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-prior/i1099mec--2024.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener">IRS 2024 instructions</a>.</p>
<p>“Office Space” can be a build-menu category. It should not be an ontology. McKibbin, Boerum, Johnson, Colorado, Porter, and Hong Kong require individual source checks before entering the scenario: physical location, lessee, operator, legal entity, opening period, and actual narrative function. Hong Kong already demonstrates why site and entity must separate: the briefing describes both local production and intercompany cash/accounting (pp. 2, 22, 48).</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/two-viable-architectures"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/two-viable-architectures">Two viable architectures</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th></th>
<th>A. Operating campus</th>
<th>B. Reconstruction park</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Player role</td>
<td>Fictional operations desk with bounded authority</td>
<td>Present-day investigator arranging a reconstruction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Main verb</td>
<td>Plan and deliver work</td>
<td>Reconstruct and test an explanation</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Spatial model</td>
<td>Facilities and production capacity; project clusters</td>
<td>Stable historical sites with reconstructed work and transaction paths</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Decisions</td>
<td>Capacity, sequencing, commitments, contingency, information requests</td>
<td>Which chain to examine, which record would distinguish explanations, how to present a bounded finding</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Story delivery</td>
<td>Ordinary work develops contradictions; case records appear at relevant mechanisms</td>
<td>Historical episodes become traversable systems; records open from their objects</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Strength</td>
<td>Familiar management pleasure and consequences before exposition</td>
<td>Stronger fidelity; unresolved endpoints can stay unresolved</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Risk</td>
<td>Counterfactual choices may imply that real people could simply have prevented the outcome</td>
<td>Investigation can become matching receipts until a chapter unlocks</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Scope</td>
<td>Small authored scenario plus reusable simulation</td>
<td>Larger evidence apparatus, lighter operational simulation</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p><strong>Recommendation: test A and B with the same small episode before choosing the campaign.</strong> A is the stronger candidate for the requested game; B is the stronger reference and explainer mode. They can share objects without pretending to share outcomes.</p>
<p>In A, explicitly fictional training or alternate scenarios allow operational choices to change results. Historical case records remain fixed and inspectable. In B, agency changes what the player understands and what they can substantiate; it does not rewrite a real bank event. Avoid a historical avatar endowed with every password and hindsight. The briefing&#39;s unequal access is not a difficulty setting that a clever player should bypass.</p>
<p>A literal “client rides, finance kiosks, fraud meter” architecture is a weak third option. It is initially recognizable but turns source categories into arbitrary gameplay categories. A kiosk can represent a physical service counter if space and staffing matter. BILL is a workbench because its consequential properties are profiles, batches, states, permissions, and evidence—not its walking distance from the studio.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/rct-craft-worth-carrying-across"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/rct-craft-worth-carrying-across">RCT craft worth carrying across</a></h2>
<p>RCT&#39;s useful inheritance is <strong>diagnosable systems assembled into a place</strong>. Keep the loop of placing a capability, watching demand and throughput, inspecting a local failure, and making a bounded adjustment.</p>
<p>There is a precise historical example of a scenario rule changing the whole economic problem: Sawyer says the new <em>Loopy Landscapes</em> parks deliberately prevent raising admission, making individual attractions earn their income. Transfer that principle to different agency contracts: a fixed retainer, approval-dependent production budget, or media commitment should change what good planning means. Do not treat all incoming money as the same fare. <a href="https://www.chrissawyergames.com/faq1.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Sawyer FAQ</a>.</p>
<p>OpenRCT2&#39;s guest implementation exposes needs, preferences, satisfaction, affordability-related behavior, and local thoughts; its ride-ratings implementation evaluates track context and proximity. These are useful inspectable mechanisms in a modern reconstruction, not proof that every current behavior exactly matches the 1999 executable. Transfer the causal specificity: “waiting for supplier confirmation” should point to a real dependency; “stressed” should not be the only diagnosis. A beautiful campus may still contain an impossible schedule. <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2/develop/src/openrct2/entity/Guest.cpp" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Guest source</a>, <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2/develop/src/openrct2/ride/RideRatings.cpp" target="_blank" rel="noopener">ratings source</a>.</p>
<p>Borrow prebuilt and editable <strong>production plans</strong>: a repeatable campaign package is analogous to a reusable ride design. The player can customize a few consequential parts—make/buy, staffing, sequence, contingency, milestones—without laying every metre of track. Sawyer&#39;s own downloadable track collection is a period example of designed objects being shared and tested in existing scenarios. <a href="https://www.chrissawyergames.com/download1.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Track collection</a>.</p>
<p>Do not import pathological incentives accidentally. If the score is revenue, the player should chase revenue even when that destroys the intended lesson. If finding fraud earns points, every ordinary payment becomes a missed scoring opportunity. Use separate outcomes: commitments honored, work delivered, liquidity maintained, people burdened, records preserved, claims supported. A bad business outcome may coexist with excellent reasoning. No overall righteousness percentage is required.</p>
<p>The user-supplied <a href="https://drew1440.com/2024/09/24/rollercoaster-tycoon/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Drew1440 RCT page</a> is a secondary visual/reference lead. The retrieved text is sparse; it has not been used to establish mechanics.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/simcity-zomboid-and-period-design-theory"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/simcity-zomboid-and-period-design-theory">SimCity, Zomboid, and period design theory</a></h2>
<p>SimCity designer Guillaume Pierre describes maps, networks/globals, agents, and building-to-building connections as different simulation techniques. That distinction is useful here: floor capacity belongs to space; obligations form a network; worker availability can be aggregated; an individual courier need only be simulated if their behavior changes a decision. There is no prize for giving every invoice pathfinding. This is an inference from his published GDC abstract, not a claim to have reviewed the complete lecture. <a href="https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1034347/We-Built-This-City-on" target="_blank" rel="noopener">GDC 2024</a>.</p>
<p>Project Zomboid offers a more useful precedent than zombies: domestic objects, persistent consequences, player-made plans, and adjustable scenario conditions. Its 2013 developer post explicitly separates canonical survival rules from customizable sandbox rules. That supports a historical record mode and a clearly marked experiment mode. It does not justify making documented uncertainty a random dice roll. <a href="https://projectzomboid.com/blog/news/2013/04/in-the-guts-of-the-sandbox/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">The Indie Stone, 2013</a>.</p>
<p>Three period/near-period tools should govern the GDD:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Doug Church, 1999:</strong> intention and perceivable consequence. Before implementing a choice, write the plan a player could form and the visible consequence that would teach them whether it worked. “Click OK to continue” satisfies neither. <a href="https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/formal-abstract-design-tools" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Formal Abstract Design Tools</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Hunicke, LeBlanc, Zubek, 2004:</strong> use mechanics/dynamics/aesthetics as a testable chain. A capacity reservation is a mechanic; juggling competing commitments is the dynamic; capable stewardship under pressure is the intended experience. Extra documents do not automatically improve that chain. <a href="https://aaai.org/papers/ws04-04-001-mda-a-formal-approach-to-game-design-and-game-research/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">MDA paper</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Henry Jenkins, 2004:</strong> embed narrative information in the environment and its objects. An idle fabrication area beside a report that claims costs vanished can create an inquiry; a popup explaining that contradiction cannot substitute for it. This is our application of narrative architecture, not an assertion about the case. <a href="https://electronicbookreview.com/publications/game-design-as-narrative-architecture/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Game Design as Narrative Architecture</a>.</li>
</ul>
<p>Bogost&#39;s procedural rhetoric adds the necessary criticism: rules themselves make arguments. Making every clearing transaction harmful would teach that clearing is inherently fraudulent, whatever the footnote says. Elizabeth Sampat&#39;s GDC abstract similarly treats simulation design as an expression of assumptions. The GDD needs a short <strong>argument made by this rule</strong> field and a counterexample beside each financial mechanism. <a href="https://bogost.com/books/persuasive-games" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Bogost</a>, <a href="https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1023994/Systems-Make-Statements-Simulations-and" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Sampat</a>.</p>
<p>Stone Librande&#39;s <em>Simulating a City, One Page at a Time</em> is a useful documentation precedent: a large design needs inspectable subsystem pages. Use a comprehensive GDD index plus one page per loop, state machine, and source-to-mechanic mapping; do not attempt to make every detail live in one immaculate monolith. Only the published session abstract was reviewed. <a href="https://www.gdcvault.com/play/1018764/Simulating-a-City-One-Page" target="_blank" rel="noopener">GDC 2013</a>.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/proposed-operating-and-inquiry-loops"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/proposed-operating-and-inquiry-loops">Proposed operating and inquiry loops</a></h2>
<p>The ordinary loop is <strong>accept scope → reserve capacity → commit suppliers → produce → obtain acceptance → collect/pay → review</strong>. Capacity, cash, and settlement move on different clocks. Departments should have skills and finite time; payroll and benefits recur independently of client collection. External engagements add flexible capacity but also lead times, milestones, approval conditions, and obligations. Neither should be universally cheaper or morally preferred.</p>
<p>The inquiry loop interrupts only when it changes a decision: <strong>notice conflict → compare representations → name the missing evidence → request or inspect it → narrow the finding → revise an operating plan</strong>. Do not require every bill to become a puzzle. Batch ordinary supported work and surface meaningful exceptions.</p>
<p>The fictional test scenario gives Campaign C-07 a $100,000 budget, an internal team, and a fabrication supplier. A $12,000 obligation is marked Paid; the supplied bank events show a debit and an equal return; no subsequent settlement is supplied. A deadline approaches. Proposed operating choices are to reserve substitute capacity, protect another project&#39;s slot, request supplier confirmation, continue with contingency, or defer the handoff. These choices and their economic consequences are not implemented in the art study. A confirmation response may be pending or unavailable: incomplete observation does not establish nonpayment.</p>
<p>The decision spends scarce capacity or cash to keep a promise while resolving uncertainty. A normal pooled-payment example must accompany the exception. The current <a href="#art/interface-materials">document workbench</a> demonstrates the fictional invoice, saved payment versions and bank-event strip. The Creative Peaks comparison has a separate <a href="#binding-inventory">export-level binding</a>: its return date differs from the briefing, the Chase extraction conflicts with QBO on direction, and the final supplier endpoint remains unresolved.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/evidence-to-mechanic-table"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/evidence-to-mechanic-table">Evidence-to-mechanic table</a></h2>
<p>All historical descriptions below are briefing-reported. Candidate mechanics are design proposals.</p>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>

<h2 id="research-systems/discovery-order-and-views"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/discovery-order-and-views">Discovery order and views</a></h2>
<p>Introduce <strong>one job</strong>, then <strong>one payment lifecycle</strong>, then <strong>multiple representations</strong>, then <strong>permissions</strong>, then <strong>affiliates and burden</strong>, then <strong>migration and afterlife</strong>. This is a learning order, not a rewritten chronology. Each later case episode retains its actual dates. A 1990s desktop presentation is an interface convention; it must not move a later chat, bank product, or AI conversation into the 1990s.</p>
<p>Use five coordinated views in this order: <strong>Work</strong> (capacity and dependencies), <strong>Cash</strong> (observed movements, attempts and unresolved endpoints), <strong>Information</strong> (versions, sources and claims), <strong>Control</strong> (dated permissions), and <strong>People</strong> (employment, engagements and workload). Rights and obligations remain in the inspector across views. Keep the selected project, person or obligation pinned while switching. An additional comparison can show the company as presented to a specified audience. “Reported sales” must name its report and period. The <a href="#interface/five-views">five-view figure</a> uses one fictional obligation to demonstrate the distinction; the art explorer presents architecture and sources, with the practice-document desk accessed separately.</p>
<p>Real named characters need source-based roles and documented acts. Use invented archetypes only in the fictional scenario: the producer protecting a deadline, the specialist waiting on a deposit, the account lead selling tomorrow&#39;s capacity. Their behaviors should have mundane operational causes. A named person&#39;s alleged culpability is not an NPC trait.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/mods-borrow-the-architecture-check-the-asset"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/mods-borrow-the-architecture-check-the-asset">Mods: borrow the architecture, check the asset</a></h2>
<p>The linked Nexus resource is <strong>Item Icons — Modders Resource</strong>, by Incariuz: 341 custom icons plus four templates. Its stated permission confines use to Project Zomboid mods, and some assets modify the game&#39;s own art. It is a craft reference, not a ready asset pack for this standalone game. <a href="https://www.nexusmods.com/projectzomboid/mods/37" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Author page</a>.</p>
<p>The linked Workshop item is <strong>Daddy Dirkie dirks tiles</strong>. It functions as a dependency for maps rather than changing the standalone game itself. The description allows credited map use but prohibits redistribution/editing without permission; later author comments discuss community continuation, which is not a clear standalone-game license. Treat it as a reference for coherent environmental kits and dependency design. <a href="https://steamcommunity.com/workshop/filedetails/?id=2337452747" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Workshop author listing</a>.</p>
<p>Plan separately for visual packs, fictional scenario packs, and rule experiments. Historical evidence packs need source/version identity and must not be silently overwritten by a mod. OpenRCT2&#39;s documented script/plugin extension is a useful example of adding behavior around a stable simulation; it is not a recommendation to build this case on the RCT engine. <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2/develop/distribution/scripting/scripting.md" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Official scripting documentation</a>.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/next-steps-prototype-gates-and-failure-tests"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/next-steps-prototype-gates-and-failure-tests">Next steps: prototype gates and failure tests</a></h2>
<p>These are proposed acceptance criteria, not measured results.</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Ordinary-play test:</strong> give six participants a three-minute production scenario without anomalies. At least four voluntarily try a second plan, and can explain its tradeoff. Failure means the management loop needs work before more lore.</li>
<li><strong>Ontology test:</strong> after the slice, at least five distinguish client, project, site, supplier, and entity without coaching. Any recurring conflation is a model or representation failure.</li>
<li><strong>Settlement test:</strong> present returned-payment, legitimate pooled-payment, and duplicate-Paid examples. At least five correctly separate observation from inference in all three. No UI state may assert a recipient without its required record.</li>
<li><strong>False-join test:</strong> include the source&#39;s two same-date $25,000 transfers (p. 45). The model must retain two objects; amount/date matching alone must never merge them.</li>
<li><strong>Control test:</strong> ask who can view, initiate, approve, and administer one transaction at two dates. At least five answer from the control view without reading an organization chart.</li>
<li><strong>Agency test:</strong> two different capacity plans must produce materially different delivery/cash consequences in fiction; neither may change documentary facts. If outcomes differ only in dialogue, the game is still a slideshow.</li>
<li><strong>Nonadditivity test:</strong> combine statement, bank, bill, and return views. The system must refuse a total across overlapping populations and explain the selected measure in plain language.</li>
<li><strong>Attention test:</strong> an eight-minute session allows at least three consequential operational actions before opening a long document. Essential facts remain recoverable without timed reading or mandatory pixel hunting.</li>
</ol>
<p>The unresolved design question is whether ordinary production planning remains enjoyable when the payment exception is removed. Test that and the evidence distinctions together before fixing the campaign structure. Completed art components can support the test without predetermining its answer.</p>
<h2 id="research-systems/research-access-and-scope"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-systems/research-access-and-scope">Research access and scope</a></h2>
<p>All linked public sources accessed 16 September 2026. External research used game/design/tax terminology only; no private case text was searched or uploaded. The primary case review was local. Public source scopes: Sawyer pages are creator statements; OpenRCT2 files are the current <code>develop</code> reconstruction and must be pinned to a revision before implementation; Indie Stone is a historical developer account; Church, MDA, Jenkins, and Bogost are author-authored design/theory works; GDC citations support their published abstracts only; mod pages support the creators&#39; descriptions and posted permissions; the IRS link is explicitly the 2024 edition. Drew1440 is secondary commentary. None of these sources independently validates the case allegations.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#evidence"><small>Previous</small>Evidence</a><a href="#research-ux"><small>Next</small>Interface research →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-research-ux" hidden aria-labelledby="title-research-ux"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">REFERENCE / R2</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-research-ux" tabindex="-1">Interface research</h1></header><div class="source-preface"><p>Research checked against design 0.10 / art 0.9, 16 September 2026. Paired exterior/interior previews, a consistent source inspector, scenic controls and a separate practice-document desk are implemented. The five-view relationship demonstration remains in the Interface chapter. The three paths below specify the operating prototype still to test; they do not describe a completed game or settle the player’s role.</p>
</div><nav class="page-links" aria-label="In Interface research"><a href="#research-ux/1-start-with-work-then-reveal-what-each-system-says-about-it"><span>01</span>1. Start with work, then reveal what each system says about it</a><a href="#research-ux/2-evidence-boundary"><span>02</span>2. BILL belongs in the workbench</a><a href="#research-ux/8-a-desktop-worth-using"><span>08</span>8. A desktop worth using</a><a href="#research-ux/9-next-steps-operating-prototype-tests"><span>09</span>9. Next steps: operating-prototype tests</a></nav><div class="release-note">DESIGN 0.10 / ART 0.9<br><a href="previews/index.html">Current previews ↗</a><br><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">Object catalogue ↗</a><br><a href="archive/index.html">Archive ↗</a></div><div class="document-tools"><a href="research/ux.md">Editable source ↗</a></div></aside><div class="chapter-main"><article class="article-body"><p class="reference-status">Supporting research and binding notes. Current design decisions are maintained in the game, interface and build chapters. Source limits remain attached to each claim.</p><h2 id="research-ux/1-start-with-work-then-reveal-what-each-system-says-about-it"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/1-start-with-work-then-reveal-what-each-system-says-about-it">1. Start with work, then reveal what each system says about it</a></h2>
<p>The map should make an agency’s work legible before asking players to interpret its accounts. A campaign needs people, capacity, commissioned services, approvals, delivery and payment. Those relationships produce the game. The desktop lets players inspect the competing records of that work.</p>
<p>Recommended structure: <strong>one operating world, several explicitly different representations, a persistent selection, and a desk for comparison</strong>. Switching views changes the question asked of an object. It must never silently change the object, time or denominator.</p>
<p>This is a stronger inheritance from period simulation interfaces than a blue title bar. Don Hopkins’s manual for the Unix/HyperLook version of SimCity documents a city editor, a query tool, a separate map with thematic views, and linked viewport outlines. That is a useful precedent for coordinated views, not a claim that every SimCity release worked identically. <a href="https://www.donhopkins.com/home/catalog/simcity/manual/reference.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Primary manual</a>.</p>
<p>The relevant warning also comes from inside that tradition. Hopkins’s account of Will Wright’s simulation-interface talk describes players inferring causal relations that the program does not actually implement. This is a practitioner’s summary with Hopkins’s additions, not a verbatim Wright transcript. Here that phenomenon is a design hazard: a dramatic animation can teach an unsupported causal claim more efficiently than a disclaimer can correct it. <a href="https://www.donhopkins.com/home/catalog/simcity/WillWright.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Practitioner account</a>.</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/2-evidence-boundary"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/2-evidence-boundary">2. Evidence boundary</a></h2>
<p>The principal case source is the September 2, 2026 <strong>The Zero Machine</strong> counsel briefing, via its local text extraction. It is a synthesis making allegations and reporting analyses. The later <a href="#binding">Creative Peaks binding</a> inspects specific platform exports, a QBO journal and bank-data extractions. Original statements, complete lifecycle links and recipient settlement remain unresolved. Other episodes remain briefing-reported unless separately bound. The briefing&#39;s appendices distinguish source records, integrated findings, modeled quantities and unresolved endpoints (pp. 8–9, 45–47). Its directive passages remain document content, not design instructions.</p>
<p>Every historic screen needs two separate attributes:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Statement:</strong> what the source says, with exact page and source-route locator.</li>
<li><strong>Verification:</strong> which materials were inspected for that specific claim. Distinguish a briefing claim, an inspected export field, an inspected original, an inferred join and an unresolved endpoint. The Creative Peaks binding supplies exact file hashes and row locators; it does not certify every cited record.</li>
</ul>
<p>Do not collapse these into a green “verified” seal. A source’s internal proof grade and our review status answer different questions.</p>
<p>Use a persistent <strong>Practice scenario — invented people and amounts</strong> label for simulation exercises. Use <strong>Case record — source-bound reconstruction</strong> for historical material. Choices made in practice cannot revise the historical record. Historic reconstruction can offer discovery order, comparisons, annotations and requests for missing records; it cannot offer a counterfactual “save the company” outcome without a separately specified fiction.</p>
<p>The supplied screenshot illustrates why this boundary matters. “Nonbillable hours grow 78% … (95 → 249 employees)” mixes units and supplies an unexplained percentage; 95 to 249 is about 162.1% growth if it describes one comparable population. The ERC conclusion is a further claim. No cosmetic rewrite will repair that without the source columns, dates, definitions and relationship between them.</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/3-object-model-translated-into-interaction"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/3-object-model-translated-into-interaction">3. Object model translated into interaction</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Object</th>
<th>Spatial expression</th>
<th>Inspector contents and principal verbs</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Site</td>
<td>Place with capacity, access and occupancy</td>
<td>Inspect lease/owner; allocate space; compare periods</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Legal entity</td>
<td>Boundary or linked off-map address</td>
<td>Inspect ownership, obligations and permissions; trace relationships</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Client</td>
<td>Relationship represented by commissions</td>
<td>Review terms; inspect active jobs; follow receivables</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Project/job</td>
<td>Temporary production cluster within sites</td>
<td>Staff, sequence, commission, deliver; inspect associated records</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Employee</td>
<td>Member of a department with commitments</td>
<td>Allocate work; inspect workload and employment records</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Supplier/freelancer</td>
<td>Counterparty linked to commissioned work</td>
<td>Request service; inspect obligation, identity and payment lifecycle</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Obligation</td>
<td>Commitment attached to work and counterparties</td>
<td>Inspect terms; compare invoice, payment and settlement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Payment attempt</td>
<td>Dated event with its own identity</td>
<td>Trace funding, return, void, reissue and settlement</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Record/version</td>
<td>Inspectable document or system object</td>
<td>Open, compare, cite, pin; inspect origin and changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Permission grant</td>
<td>Time-bounded relationship to a system/action</td>
<td>Show who could view, approve, execute, edit, export or revoke</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>These categories may share artwork, but must not share identity. A freelancer can walk into the park like a visitor; economically they remain a supplier of work. A client representative is a visitor too, with a different relationship. “Guest” is therefore a presentation category, not the root of the financial model. A 1099 belongs to a tax-reporting relationship and period; it should not be a universal wearable accessory assigned from a character’s silhouette.</p>
<p>Similarly, an office is not a ride because it appears in an important episode. Place it when location, occupancy, lease burden or access changes play. An entity might own or occupy several sites, while a site may host several entities. Give shared-control relationships readable boundaries without implying geographic proximity establishes control.</p>
<p>A permission grant is a separate object because a job title cannot substitute for access. The briefing’s responsibility-versus-authority passages make this distinction particularly important (pp. 10–16).</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/4-the-five-views"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/4-the-five-views">4. The five views</a></h2>
<div class="table-scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable specification table"><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>View</th>
<th>Main question</th>
<th>Marks shown</th>
<th>Explicit limits</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td>Work</td>
<td>What is happening and what blocks delivery?</td>
<td>Project stages, dependencies, queues, capacity</td>
<td>Activity is not revenue or cash</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Cash</td>
<td>What transfer is observed, attempted or unresolved?</td>
<td>Bank events and payment lifecycles; a separate book-state strip</td>
<td>Paid is a platform label, not settlement proof</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Information</td>
<td>What record exists, where did it come from, what changed?</td>
<td>Documents, lineage, versions and missing links</td>
<td>A missing export is not evidence of deletion</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Control</td>
<td>Who could do which action, in which system, at that time?</td>
<td>Explicit permission edges and approval gates</td>
<td>Capability is not proof of an action or physical-user identity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>People</td>
<td>Who does the work and bears the interruption?</td>
<td>Department load, commitments, service dependencies</td>
<td>Do not infer real people’s emotions, knowledge or motives</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></div>
<p>Persistent header: selected object, viewed period, scenario type, active view. Time has two coordinates when necessary: <strong>event date</strong> and <strong>record version/as-of date</strong>. The bank event may be old while its platform representation changes later. A single date slider cannot faithfully express both.</p>
<p>The view switch retains selection, camera, zoom, period, pinned comparisons and navigation history. If an object has no representation in a view, keep its inspector open and say why. Do not drop it or quietly substitute its parent. A breadcrumb reads, for example, <code>Project C-07 / Obligation O-19 / Attempt P-04</code>.</p>
<p>Use solid connectors for supported relationships, dashed connectors for hypotheses, and visible broken ends for missing links. Explain every connector in words on selection. Do not animate speculative cash to an inferred beneficiary. Beyond bank-confirmed settlement, terminate at the last supported address.</p>
<p>Distinguish <strong>zero</strong>, <strong>not recorded</strong>, <strong>not supplied</strong>, <strong>not applicable</strong>, <strong>modeled</strong> and <strong>not yet inspected</strong>. In particular, the briefing’s 2024 input is N/A while its computation uses a zero for a limited model purpose (p. 9). Those must not become the same empty cell.</p>
<p>Totals require a population label, unit, period and basis. Selecting overlapping populations does not produce an automatic grand total. State “These selections overlap” and offer a deduplicated episode view. The briefing expressly prohibits adding its parallel statement, payment, bank and mutation surfaces as damages (pp. 9, 46).</p>
<p>Shneiderman’s 1996 visual-information taxonomy includes relation, history and extraction as well as overview, zoom, filtering and detail. Those less-quoted operations matter here: players need to preserve a trail and take a comparison away, not simply reveal another tooltip. <a href="https://www.cs.umd.edu/users/ben/papers/Shneiderman1996eyes.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Original paper</a>.</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/5-three-core-paths"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/5-three-core-paths">5. Three core paths</a></h2>
<h3 id="research-ux/path-a-make-one-campaign-work"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/path-a-make-one-campaign-work">Path A — Make one campaign work</a></h3>
<p><strong>Purpose:</strong> establish the ordinary economy before introducing an anomaly. <strong>Setting:</strong> synthetic practice scenario, invented $100,000 client budget.</p>
<ol>
<li>Accept a small commission with three deliverables and a visible due date. Open its terms in place; no introductory accounting lecture.</li>
<li>Place a project cluster inside available studio space. Compare in-house production, which occupies staff and equipment, with an independent fabricator’s $12,000 component and longer lead time. Preview capacity and contractual commitments first. This worked example follows fabrication; the in-house alternative must remain a viable plan in the playtest.</li>
<li>Run one planning interval. The world responds: the production queue clears or backs up, a handoff succeeds or waits, the department load changes. The player’s arrangement remains visible.</li>
<li>Inspect a delay. The work inspector names the dependency and offers <code>Reschedule</code>, <code>Reserve alternative capacity</code>, or <code>Inspect obligation</code>.</li>
<li>Deliver the first component. Compare completed work, invoiced work and received money on one short receipt. They need not move together.</li>
</ol>
<p>Pleasure comes from arranging a small working system and watching it function. Authored choices concern sequence, slack and resource commitments, not every footpath. The first lesson is that productive activity has its own identity; the accounts are representations of it.</p>
<h3 id="research-ux/path-b-a-payment-returned-the-interface-still-says-paid"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/path-b-a-payment-returned-the-interface-still-says-paid">Path B — A payment returned; the interface still says Paid</a></h3>
<p><strong>Purpose:</strong> discriminate states, choose a useful next record, and manage the operational consequence. Continue the synthetic campaign.</p>
<ol>
<li>An unobtrusive project notice says the fabricated component is held. Its obligation shows platform status <code>Paid</code>, with settlement <code>Not yet checked</code>.</li>
<li>Pin the obligation and open the payment workbench. Compare a $12,000 debit, an exact $12,000 return and the surviving Paid label. Three facts; no verdict button.</li>
<li>Link the return to the existing attempt. The lifecycle now reads <code>Attempted → returned</code>; the obligation remains open for resolution. The game does not count a second loss or fabricate another payment.</li>
<li>Choose the next information request: processor history, recipient confirmation or an unrelated invoice export. Each explains what it can establish; responses can be pending, unavailable or confirmed. Missing settlement evidence alone does not prove nonpayment.</li>
<li>Choose an operational response: reserve alternative capacity for an option fee, defer the handoff, or revise the production sequence. Keep available cash, deadline and supplier commitment separately visible. Delays and tradeoffs belong to this practice model; they imply no historical opportunity to rescue the company.</li>
<li>Encounter a legitimate pooled funding example. A correctly supported batch must remain ordinary even though several records share one bank debit. Reward the accurate distinction, not the number of suspicious items found.</li>
</ol>
<p>The narrative path then requires a short documentary comparison; the longer dossier remains optional. The briefing’s Creative Peaks episode reports a $23,500 debit and return with PaidInFull retained (p. 34). Its <a href="#binding-inventory">inspected exports</a> support specific fields but leave the October 21/22 return-date conflict, Chase-direction conflict, complete return linkage and final receipt unresolved. The Reddit example—$50,000 of payment stories around one $25,000 debit—remains a briefing claim in this design work. The cited corrections include ordinary pooled allocations and bank-supported pairs (pp. 45–46). A playable control needs its own binding; the export-level $4,520 pooled comparison has not yet met that gate.</p>
<h3 id="research-ux/path-c-same-work-different-record"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/path-c-same-work-different-record">Path C — Same work, different record</a></h3>
<p><strong>Purpose:</strong> understand control and lineage without making bookkeeping a memorization test. <strong>Setting:</strong> source-bound historical reconstruction once exhibits are available; synthetic substitute until then.</p>
<ol>
<li>Select a project and compare two dated presentations. Their totals or identities differ; the selected economic work stays fixed.</li>
<li>Open Information view. Pin source and successor side by side, highlighting retained fields and missing fields separately.</li>
<li>Switch to Control with the same record selected. See title, responsibility and granted actions in separate rows. The useful question becomes “Who could export this version?” rather than “Who is the villain?”</li>
<li>Inspect an audit event. A named account is labeled an account; person attribution stays unresolved unless separately supported. Request authentication/session evidence when needed.</li>
<li>Assemble a short finding from linked observations: <code>These fields changed</code>; <code>this account is recorded</code>; <code>this question remains open</code>. The interface blocks no opinion, but keeps personal annotations visually separate from source statements.</li>
<li>Return to the same place and time on the map. A bookmark stores the comparison and can be reopened or exported with citations.</li>
</ol>
<p>This path gives research actions consequence: a better-supported explanation and a navigable reconstruction. It should not pretend that discovering one field grants the power to reverse a real migration or prevent a historical closure.</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/6-low-fidelity-screens"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/6-low-fidelity-screens">6. Low-fidelity screens</a></h2>
<p>These are planning wireframes for the proposed operating paths. The current <a href="#art/world-and-interface">world and inspector</a> and <a href="#art/interface-materials">document specimens</a> show the implemented art-study treatment. At desktop size the map and inspector are co-visible. The current document reader pauses motion and covers the map with a return control. The operating prototype still needs to test the comparison layout and narrower-screen reading path.</p>
<pre><code class="language-text">WORKSPACE · Practice scenario                   [Pause] [Next interval]
Project C-07     Week 3     [Work Cash Information Control People]
┌───────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
│                                   │ C-07 / Fabricated component  │
│ Studio + production clusters      │ Waiting on supplier release  │
│ Selected cluster outlined         │                              │
│ Department activity is visible    │ Due: Thursday                │
│                                   │ Obligation: $12,000           │
│ [mini-map]                        │ Platform: Paid               │
│                                   │ Settlement: Not yet checked  │
│                                   │ [Inspect obligation]         │
│                                   │ [Reserve capacity]           │
└───────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

</code></pre>
<pre><code class="language-text">PAYMENT WORKBENCH · O-19 · P-04            Practice / Week 3 / Paused
┌──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐

[Link return] [Request record] [Pin comparison] [Back to project]

</code></pre>

┌──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐

└──────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘
[Information lineage] [Permissions at this date] [Audit event]
Observed difference: project field absent from the later version

Personal note: editable      [Save comparison] [Return to selection]
</code></pre>
<h2 id="research-ux/7-bill-belongs-in-the-workbench"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/7-bill-belongs-in-the-workbench">7. BILL belongs in the workbench</a></h2>
<p>Bill payment is an instrument acting on obligations. An office or finance department is a place/capacity/control arrangement. Confusing them would imply that walking to a kiosk determines payment authority, or that buying another kiosk repairs missing settlement evidence.</p>
<p>A finance building can be a useful map entry point, with a visible queue if review capacity is part of the simulation. Every relevant obligation should still open the same workbench directly. Software administration, approval, execution and bank authority are distinct gates within it. Account 1072 must appear as a ledger clearing account, never a cash-storage tank or bank balance; the briefing explicitly makes that distinction (p. 1).</p>
<p>Beaudouin-Lafon’s 2000 instrumental-interaction model distinguishes domain objects from the tools mediating action on them. This gives a concrete test: an obligation is the object; comparison, tracing and payment scheduling are tools; a building represents a place or capability. <a href="https://www.lri.fr/~mbl/papers/CHI2000/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Author’s paper</a>.</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/8-a-desktop-worth-using"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/8-a-desktop-worth-using">8. A desktop worth using</a></h2>
<p>Use a shallow desk with three arrangements: <code>World + inspector</code>, <code>Compare</code>, and <code>Reading</code>. Let players pin a small set of documents, save an arrangement and return without cleaning up a pile of windows. Kandogan and Shneiderman’s Elastic Windows research explored grouping and manipulating related windows; its 1995 scenario is a relevant period precedent for workspace organization. Our proposal is restrained tiling, not a reproduction. <a href="https://www.cs.umd.edu/projects/hcil/elastic-windows/scenario.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Primary scenario</a>.</p>
<p>Documents arrive through a visible task, colleague, record request or archive location. Each acquisition changes what the player can inspect; it need not trigger a modal. Quotes such as the referenced “no yappin” messages are discoverable source artifacts only after the actual record is supplied. Do not manufacture the message, its sender, date, context or significance from the prompt.</p>
<p>Use a small contextual action menu with stable verbs—Inspect, Follow, Compare, Pin—also exposed in the inspector. Hopkins’s 1998 account of his Sims interface describes object-relative action menus and a face identifying the acting character. The transferable lesson is to keep action and actor clear. A radial menu is optional and must earn its space in testing. <a href="https://www.donhopkins.com/home/catalog/piemenus/NaturalSelection.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Designer’s account</a>.</p>
<p>Density should unfold through dependency. Introduce a term when the player needs it, offer a one-line definition, and keep the fuller reference one step away. Never hide the qualification necessary to read a number correctly. Advanced controls may wait; “modeled” cannot.</p>
<h2 id="research-ux/9-next-steps-operating-prototype-tests"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-ux/9-next-steps-operating-prototype-tests">9. Next steps: operating-prototype tests</a></h2>
<p>Build paper or clickable-screen tests of the three paths, using synthetic fixtures. Recruit six to eight participants spanning management-game familiarity and financial familiarity; use results diagnostically, not as a statistically representative verdict.</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>State discrimination:</strong> include paid-and-settled, paid-but-returned, legitimate pooled funding, duplicate records without duplicate cash, and unresolved endpoints. Can players explain each without inferring theft from a label? Any systematic error stops content production.</li>
<li><strong>View continuity:</strong> ask participants to follow one obligation across all five views, then return to the original project. Record lost selections, mistaken dates and mistaken object substitutions.</li>
<li><strong>Playable agency:</strong> first test three minutes with no anomaly. Allow two valid production plans with different costs. Ask players to identify consequences without reading a final explanation. If all meaningful action occurs in notices, the loop needs redesign.</li>
<li><strong>Source discipline:</strong> ask what was established by an exhibit, asserted by the briefing, inferred by the player, and invented for practice. Confusing these categories is a release blocker.</li>
<li><strong>Retention:</strong> the following day, ask for a sketch of work → obligation → payment attempt → settlement → record. The target is a usable distinction, not recall of names or totals.</li>
</ol>
<p>Provide keyboard operation and a nonspatial object list alongside the map. Reading and comparison pause simulation by default. Scale text independently of sprites; never force pixel fonts on long documents. Use labels/patterns as well as color, reduced motion, adjustable sound, visible focus and no hover-only evidence. The interface can look composed and particular without making accuracy depend on perfect vision, fast reading or a steady mouse.</p>
<p>The immediate design gate is small: can someone run one piece of work, discover one record conflict, and choose a sensible response while maintaining those distinctions? Until that works, a larger park will mostly give the confusion somewhere to walk.</p>

<h2 id="research-refinement/art-09-corrections"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-refinement/art-09-corrections">Art 0.9 corrections</a></h2>
<p>Supernova keeps its pavilion and orbital ornament, with a black body, chrome canopy, ribbed silver panels and bright fascia lettering. Johnson now follows the supplied loading-door frontage. Its interior has a stair descending from the mezzanine, a central white birch and the office cat. The scene clears the foreground sightline to that interior. The original 0.8 review is retained in the version archive.</p>

<h2 id="research-refinement/craft-references"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-refinement/craft-references">Craft references</a></h2>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.chrissawyergames.com/feature3.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Chris Sawyer / graphics up close</a>: detailed models rendered down to the game’s scale. Applied here as a form-and-lighting principle, with original code-drawn geometry.</li>
<li><a href="https://markinns.com/archive/isometric-pixel-art-car.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Mark Inns / isometric car tutorial, 2009</a>: broad body shading, selective highlights and checking at native size. Applied to the sedan’s panel hierarchy and chrome.</li>
<li><a href="https://pixeljoint.com/pixelart/47592.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Balamoot / Set of carz 2.0, 2009</a>: a period isometric vehicle catalogue. Research reference only; no pixels or source assets copied.</li>
</ul>
<p>The reception is a small curved mesh rasterized with surface normals. Car panels use per-pixel depth to prevent trim and glazing from being covered by a later body polygon. Other objects use shaped raster primitives and face-specific ramps. Static sprites are cached at native resolution; labels and document controls remain live HTML. This is still a one-camera art study, not a completed production asset set.</p>
<h2 id="research-refinement/verification"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-refinement/verification">Verification</a></h2>
<p>Every previous object is compared with the retained 0.7 renderer. Every exported frame is checked against its atlas rectangle. Bounds and source hashes are checked during export. The existing six-leg fedora test verifies facing, motion, endpoint holds and both loading orders. The GIF encoder checks dimensions, timing and sampled decoded pixels.</p>
<p>Recognition is a visual judgment, separate from these mechanical checks. The comparison sheet and live park were inspected against the photo references; 0.7 renderers are retained for review.</p>
<h2 id="research-refinement/next-steps"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-refinement/next-steps">Next steps</a></h2>
<p>Test object recognition at native scene scale, selection in overlapping areas, and the roof-on/off relationship without labels. The next operating prototype must demonstrate consequences for work and resources separately from the existing scenic animation. New asset directions, additional camera views and a larger animation set remain uncommitted.</p>
</article><nav class="chapter-footer" aria-label="Chapter navigation"><a href="#research-ux"><small>Previous</small>Interface research</a><a href="#research-offices"><small>Next</small>Office architecture →</a></nav></div></section><section class="reader-page" id="page-research-offices" hidden aria-labelledby="title-research-offices"><aside class="chapter-notes"><div class="note-id">REFERENCE / R4</div><header class="chapter-heading"><h1 id="title-research-offices" tabindex="-1">Office architecture</h1></header><div class="source-preface"><p>Research checked against design 0.10 / art 0.9, 16 September 2026. The accepted pixel system now includes office-derived architectural shells, cutaways and furnishings. This brief records their source basis and remaining spatial tests. The companion Porter and Boerum studies are architectural studies; the catalogue and assembled park show the current reusable assets.</p>
</div><nav class="page-links" aria-label="In Office architecture"><a href="#research-offices/architectural-direction"><span>01</span>Architectural direction</a><a href="#research-offices/johnson-frontage-and-interior-correction"><span>02</span>Johnson frontage and interior correction</a><a href="#research-offices/evidence-and-attribution"><span>03</span>Evidence and attribution</a><a href="#research-offices/four-architectural-references"><span>04</span>Four architectural references</a><a href="#research-offices/boerum-street-reference"><span>05</span>Boerum street reference</a><a href="#research-offices/what-the-component-kit-takes-from-the-photographs"><span>06</span>What the component kit takes from the photographs</a><a href="#research-offices/colour-and-material-rules"><span>07</span>Colour and material rules</a><a href="#research-offices/next-steps-spatial-and-recognition-tests"><span>08</span>Next steps: spatial and recognition tests</a><a href="#research-offices/supporting-inspected-images-and-research-record"><span>09</span>Supporting inspected images and research record</a></nav><div class="release-note">DESIGN 0.10 / ART 0.9<br><a href="previews/index.html">Current previews ↗</a><br><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">Object catalogue ↗</a><br><a href="archive/index.html">Archive ↗</a></div><div class="document-tools"><a href="research/office-art-research.md">Editable source ↗</a></div></aside><div class="chapter-main"><article class="article-body"><p class="reference-status">Supporting research and binding notes. Current design decisions are maintained in the game, interface and build chapters. Source limits remain attached to each claim.</p><h2 id="research-offices/architectural-direction"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-offices/architectural-direction">Architectural direction</a></h2>
<p>The current buildings use long masonry shells, tall metal window grids, flat roof planes and deliberate interior insertions. The photographs supply distinctive forms: orange stair rails, timber room-boxes, an indoor tree, booth seating and a white car. The current kit gives the three building families distinct facades and cutaways rather than assigning architecture by department.</p>
<p>The shared projection, person/door scale, hard pixel edges, modular construction and material ramps remain. The park compresses and recombines the architecture to leave room for attractions and visible circulation. Its composite layout is an invention, not a reconstruction of geographically adjacent offices.</p>

<p>The same footprint supports both states. The catalogue supplies <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#B-01">Boerum</a>, <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#B-02">Porter</a> and <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#B-03">Johnson</a> at native scale and enlarged for inspection.</p>
<h2 id="research-offices/johnson-frontage-and-interior-correction"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-offices/johnson-frontage-and-interior-correction">Johnson frontage and interior correction</a></h2>
<figure class="inline-example"><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html"><img src="assets/office-references/johnson-street-2017.png" alt="266 Johnson, supplied Street View from September 2017"></a><figcaption>266 Johnson, supplied Street View from September 2017<a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html">Inspect catalogue ↗</a></figcaption></figure>

A <a href="assets/office-references/johnson-street-2019.png">July 2019 capture</a> shows the same frontage. Capture dates do not establish dates of office occupation.</p>
<figure class="inline-example"><a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#B-03"><img src="assets/sprite-catalogue/objects/B-03/roof-off.png" alt="266 Johnson, current interior"></a><figcaption>266 Johnson, current interior<a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#B-03">Inspect B-03 ↗</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The orange stair descends from the mezzanine into the hall. The white birch sits in the central clearing; the cat occupies the front floor. The birch identification, former marble-warehouse context and adjoining art gallery are supplied project context. No gallery identity or tenancy date is assigned. The compact footprint, unseen roof and furniture layout are park interpretations. <a href="assets/sprite-catalogue/index.html#B-03">Exterior, interior and exports</a>.</p>
<h2 id="research-offices/evidence-and-attribution"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-offices/evidence-and-attribution">Evidence and attribution</a></h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>65 Porter — primary public + local.</strong> <a href="https://mattfryed.com/65porter" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Matt Fry’s project page</a> identifies his work across concept, interiors, architectural design, fabrication management and art, and separately credits PSF Projects Architecture DPC as architects. <a href="https://www.psfprojects.com/workplace/madwell-creative-agency" target="_blank" rel="noopener">PSF’s project page</a> credits its team, Millwright’s millwork collaboration, and photographer Zach Pontz. These are compatible contributions, not competing claims. The portfolio lists 2019–2021 and 21,000 sq ft; the architect lists 22,000 sq ft; <a href="https://www.workdesign.com/2023/10/take-a-tour-of-madwells-brooklyn-offices-by-psf-projects/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Work Design’s 2023 project report</a> lists completion in 2022. Keep the scopes attached to their sources; none is needed as an exact sprite dimension.</li>
<li><strong>266 Johnson — primary public + local.</strong> <a href="https://mattfryed.com/266-johnson" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Matt’s project page</a> describes adapting an adjacent marble-storage warehouse after growth at Boerum, with custom desks/furniture and his oversight of construction. It lists interior design, design direction and project management, 2015–2016. The project page does not state a photographer. Filenames elsewhere containing a photographer’s name are insufficient to assign every image in the set.</li>
<li><strong>Boerum — supplied location identification; interiors still unresolved.</strong> The supplied street-view screenshot identifies three adjoining brick-and-glass frontages as the Boerum offices. It provides a facade reference: brick lower walls, pale metal glazing grids, tall upper glass, narrow entries and a sidewalk tree. The capture date, measured footprint and full roof layout remain unknown. The fedora-clad pedestrian is an invented scene vignette; no identity or case role is inferred. <a href="https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/north-brooklyn-vision-plan/north-brooklyn-full-low.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener">NYC’s North Brooklyn report</a>, printed p.66 / PDF page 86, discusses the Boerum space and its adjacent expansion together. Its photograph shows white partitions with square openings and a high warehouse ceiling, strongly resembling the documented Johnson interior. It has no room/address caption. It is not adequate evidence for a separate Boerum facade or floor plan. Matt’s role at Boerum remains supplied context; a separate Boerum project credit was not located in this bounded search.</li>
</ul>
<p>The verified local archive is the locally retained Matt Fry portfolio copy. Its project JSON records map local image paths to public Squarespace source URLs. The archive is a source copy, not proof that every photograph is licensed for redistribution. Use the photographs as credited review references; make original game assets from the observations below.</p>
<h2 id="research-offices/four-architectural-references"><a class="section-anchor" href="#research-offices/four-architectural-references">Four architectural references</a></h2>
<p>The four inspected photographs establish shell, internal construction, occupation and signature landmarks. They are shown together in the <a href="#art/office-architecture">office reference board</a>; the source table retains the full-image locators and attribution.</p>
